### Phase-scoped audit corrections (2026-10-21)

This maintenance pass does not itself advance or mark any pending phase complete.
It addresses regressions in completed phases: filesystem containment and failed
mutation rollback (P-012/P-165), config undo and file relocation (P-152/P-153/P-161),
sandbox isolation/resource defaults, dependency installation and bounded logs
(P-168 through P-180), eval pins/cancellation and honest unwired-default errors
(P-322/P-323/P-324), API fallback routing (P-235), and the CI Vitest invocation
(P-316). Concurrent P-330 work is preserved; only its compilation blockers are in
scope for this maintenance pass (ADR-139).

Deferred according to the existing phase record, not silently treated as fixed:

- P-192/P-252: register the real merge handler map and wire the dashboard REST/data
  providers. Eval must fail closed until it can produce and verify a child repo;
  an empty pipeline or a build of parent B is not a measured merge.
- P-239/P-240 (still unchecked below): fence expired job owners, renew leases and
  recover interrupted `running` stages before claiming safe resume behavior.
- ADR-044: `/workspace` remains writable because build tools write into the project.
  A separate immutable input/writable-copy design is not introduced by this patch.
- P-327/P-333: the real provider-backed corpus baseline and published evaluation
  remain deferred; synthetic/injected-port checks are not a substitute.

Validation (2026-10-21): API regression RED then GREEN (2 files / 7 tests);
eval repairs 3 files / 22 tests; file-safety 4 files / 77 tests; agent-tool
undo/imports 28 tests; sandbox 124 passed + 1 Docker-gated skip. Root
`bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`, contract freeze, eval-schema `--check`,
scoped ESLint on every changed file, scripts 8/8, web typechecks, `build:web`,
browser flows 3/3 and performance 3/3 passed. Root `bun run lint` still reports
18 formatting errors confined to the concurrent P-331 taxonomy files, which this
pass does not own. The full suite is NOT green: the documented ADR-079
jobs/idempotency red and load-sensitive build/circular smoke failures remain, and
native `bun test` still fails on the existing `bunfig.toml` preload entry. No
coverage total was produced (stale foreign coverage lock; not deleted). The
API/CI changes were independently reviewed clean; the safety/sandbox and eval
reviews could not run because the review provider quota was exhausted, so those
received only parent review. No real Docker, live AI provider, or GitHub backend
was exercised. Accepted limits: ADR-044 writable `/workspace`, Docker daemon log
disk growth, best-effort import parsing, non-atomic undo compare, preflight (not
race-free) path containment, and uncooperative eval ports delaying completion.

**P-360 DONE (2026-10-24) - Epic 19 adds the `stitch_check_license` pre-merge licence due-diligence tool.** ADR-169. NEW `packages/mcp/src/tools/check-license.ts`: `stitch_check_license` takes `{repo_a, repo_b}` and returns `{verdict, spdx_ids, details}` where `verdict` is `compatible`/`conditional`/`incompatible` taken straight from the P-120 matrix decision (`allow` -> compatible, `review` -> conditional with `requirements`, `deny` -> incompatible with `blockers`), `spdx_ids` is the sorted union and `details` carries each side's source, ids, licence refs (id, SPDX-registry name, category), warnings and `cached` flag; the tool binds core only through the public barrel, via two injectable ports (`scan` = P-118 `scanDeclared` + `normalizeToSPDX` + `lookupLicense`, cloning a remote ref with P-069 `cloneRepo` and scanning a local path in place; `compatibility` = P-120 `checkCompatibility` over `DEFAULT_LICENSE_POLICY`), and `loadCheckLicensePorts()` builds the REAL ports, refusing with CONFIG_ERROR field `mcp.core` only when the barrel is missing a name. NEW `check-license.test.ts` (7 cases) + `src/tools/__fixtures__/check-license-harness.ts` (one `CHECK_LICENSE <json>` line over four fixture repos: MIT, Apache-2.0, GPL-3.0-only, Proprietary) + the mandated `packages/mcp/test_check_license.py`; `packages/mcp/src/index.ts` registers it beside the P-357 core tools and the P-358/P-359 tools (10 tools when no registry is injected). Mandated ids `mcp/test_check_license.py::test_compatible_pair` / `::test_incompatible_pair` / `::test_cache_speed` / `::test_spdx_details` GREEN (4 ok, exit 0) via `uv run --no-project python packages/mcp/test_check_license.py`; `@repo-stitcher/mcp` vitest 33/33 (4 files); root `bun run typecheck` 0; root `bun run lint` 0. Recorded honestly: a GPL pair is `conditional` (the default policy allows weak-copyleft and any allowed copyleft tier allows the others, so strong copyleft lands on `review`) and only an undeterminable licence such as a manifest declaring `Proprietary` is `incompatible`; the P-303 repo-metadata cache is NOT barrel-exported, so `cached` is a port-reported flag (always `false` from the default ports) and the mandated cache id proves the contract through an injected caching scan, with the missing core cache recorded as the deferral. The concurrent lane's uncommitted reds are not this phase's.

**P-335 DONE (2026-10-21) - gate green for the files this phase owns.** ADR-144. `packages/core/src/resolve/lsp-client.ts` (NEW, a LEAF core module: node:child_process + neverthrow + the StitchError type only) - the `LSPClient` class spawns the server over stdio, sends the initialize request and negotiates the capabilities, correlates requests with a monotonic id (a `Map<id,{resolve,timer,method}>`; an UNKNOWN id is ignored so a late response cannot break the next request; a server `error` settles with INTERNAL), handles the server notifications through a `Map<method,Set<handler>>` registry with a disposer, and shuts down gracefully (the `shutdown` REQUEST + the `exit` NOTIFICATION, wait for the exit, SIGKILL on the timeout); `isAlive()` is the health check and an unexpected exit fails the pending requests and triggers ONE auto-reconnect (the `closing` flag suppresses it during our own shutdown). The framing is `Content-Length: N\r\n\r\n{json}` with N the BYTE length; a malformed header drops the buffer instead of throwing. The client NEVER throws: every fallible step returns a Result and the errors reuse the existing StitchError codes (CONFIG_ERROR with the field `lsp.server`/`lsp.initialize` for a server that will not start, INTERNAL for a protocol violation / a timeout / a server error / a dead process; no new code). `packages/core/src/__tests__/fixtures/lsp-stub-server.ts` (a protocol-faithful hermetic stub) + `packages/core/src/__tests__/fixtures/lsp-client-harness.ts` (the bun harness the python test drives: the REAL typescript-language-server when the binary is present, else the stub; initialize + a textDocument/definition + 3 concurrent requests + the shutdown) + `packages/core/src/resolve/lsp-client.test.ts` (the 9-case vitest mirror) + `packages/core/test_lsp_client.py` (the four mandated ids VERBATIM: test_initialize / test_definition_request / test_concurrent_requests / test_graceful_shutdown) + the barrel export line + the `typescript-language-server` devDependency of packages/core. The bundled `typescript` tsserver does NOT speak LSP (its own protocol), which is why the bridge is the real server. ADR-144 records that the client is built although the P-334 gate is closed (the P-335 text is unconditional; the client is inert until a resolver uses it; the per-language resolvers P-336-P-339 stay deferred) - this supersedes ADR-143 decision 6. Gate: the 9-case core mirror GREEN, the four mandated python ids GREEN, scripts 8/8, web 39 files / 164 tests GREEN, E2E 3 passed, PERF 3 passed, the root typecheck 0 and the root lint 0 for the files this phase owns. The core suite shows three reds: the documented ADR-079 core red (jobs/idempotency > mixed stages) and TWO license tests broken by the CONCURRENT maintenance pass's unstaged license-module rewrite (packages/core/src/license/*) - a different lane's in-flight work, left unstaged and not fixed here. Key reconciliation (full detail in ADR-083): the four components are HAND-BUILT over ink's `useInput` because the installed `@inkjs/ui` 2.0's `Select` has no `onSelect`, no wrap-around (its state reducer returns unchanged at the list edges - read from `build/components/select/*`, not assumed), and no ESC channel, and stacking a second `useInput` for ESC over one of its components risks double handling; ink 7's App drives the READABLE stdin contract (`'readable'` listener + `stdin.read()` drains - an `emit('data')` fake is dead input, probe-established, so the test fake implements `setEncoding/setRawMode/ref/unref/pause/resume/read/press` and renders cast as `ReadStream & {press}`); one keyboard contract for all four (up/down wrap, space toggle, return submit or the default choice, backspace delete, esc delegates, ctrl ignored, arrow-free modifiers dropped); theme is the provisional `theme('ok')` paint and painted strings MUST live inside `<Text>` - ink 7's reconciler rejects them as raw Box children ("Text string ... must be rendered inside <Text>"), the commit then dies and every hook in the tree goes dark (input never attaches) - found live through the probe loop and wrapped before this could strand a real picker; secrets render asterisks by length only (the typed value never appears in any frame - test-asserted over all captured chunks); the wrappers gate on an injected stdout's `isTTY` first (the test seam) else `process.stdout.isTTY`, honor `--yes` as a forced non-interactive, resolve defaults off-TTY (confirm: `--yes` wins, then the default, else the conservative false - never a yes nobody gave, per P-189), and turn `require` without a default into the same typed rejection; every esc/cancel rejects typed `USER_CANCELLED {reason: 'cancelled'}` (the P-163 rule is fatal/abort, friendlyError-safe), the wrappers are async so a require-rejection is a PROMISE rejection not a synchronous throw, and the mount unmounts inside `try/finally` before resolving or rejecting; `render` options pin `interactive: true` (ink 7's `is-in-ci` detection silently disables input handling under CI and would strand a picker waiting for keys), `patchConsole: false` (avoids the node `Console` swap the house `tui.test.tsx` pattern needs) and `exitOnCtrlC: false` (the shell stays in command of the process); ESC keypresses assert through `.rejects` attached BEFORE the press (vitest reports a promise whose consumer attaches later than the rejection flushed). Non-TTY cases stub `process.stdout.isTTY` via `defineProperty` with the saved descriptor restored in `afterEach`. Migration is the reusability proof: both prompt PORTS keep their injected contracts (P-196/P-197 test surfaces untouched), `prompt.ts` is now the single prompting surface for merge wiring (P-192) and any future prompting command. PHASE WORDING vs REALITY: the phase says the key goes to "`~/.stitch/config`"; the real file is `~/.stitch/config.json` through the existing `StitchStore` â€” use the store, do not write a second file. ONE REGISTRY DETAIL: step 4 says "register in `cli.ts`", but the command registry lives in `program.ts` (`createProgram()` registers `status` + `doctor`; `cli.ts` owns only global options + the exit convention per ADR-075). `init` must never write the raw key into a scaffolded (committed) file â€” `.env.example` carries NAMES only.
**P-189 DONE (2026-10-17) â€” gate green.** Files: `packages/cli/src/{cli.ts, globals.ts}`, `index.ts` (barrel retained + guarded entrypoint), `index.test.ts`, `program.ts`. Gate: root typecheck 0, eslint 0, CLI suite **8 files / 38 tests passed**, all 5 mandated cases (`prints version`, `lists help`, `unknown command exits 1`, `parses global flags`, `maps exit codes`). THREE phase-vs-codebase conflicts were found and resolved on the record (ADR-075): (1) `program.onCommandNotFound` is a PHANTOM â€” commander 15.0.0 has no such method, so the unknown-command path is the `commander.unknownCommand` code through `exitOverride`; (2) the phase names `index.ts` a "new entrypoint" but it is an existing library barrel its own tests import â€” so it stays a barrel and the run is guarded by a direct-invocation check; (3) the phase mandates `-V, --verbose` while commander's `.version()` claims `-V` â€” version is registered long-form only (`--version`). Exit code for an unknown command is **1** (the phase's acceptance criteria and its own test name), with **2** reserved for option/argument usage faults.

> **Note on status:** This file tracks *code implementation* completion (each phase requires `bun run validate` green per AGENTS.md). The **plan document** (`PHASES_DETAILED.md`) is fully deep-elaborated (365/365 phases at 9/9 FULL). Implementation is **contiguous and gap-free for the core lane P-000 through P-238 (the P-189-P-237 CLI/Web lane is aradhy and is not implemented here)** - every phase in that range is implemented, tested, and covered by a green full gate. **Epic 7 (AI Agent Tools & Loop) is CLOSED.** **Epic 8 (Sandbox, P-168-P-180) is CLOSED.** **Epic 9 (Provenance, P-181-P-188) is CLOSED; P-189 opens the CLI.** Next phase: P-189.

| 2026-10-16 | P-238 pipeline | **P-238** | The deterministic core of Wave 3. `pipeline/states.ts` (9 `PIPELINE_STAGES`, 7 `STAGE_STATUSES`, the `TRANSITIONS` table, `canTransition`, `initialRecords`/`pendingRecord`, `nextStage`/`stageOrder`, `isTerminal`+`isPaused`+`isSettledStatus`, `StageRecord`); `pipeline/stateMachine.ts` (`PipelineStateMachine` with `transition`/`run`/`records`/`record`, plus `resumePoint`/`resetStage` and the `PipelineEvent`/`StageOutcome`/`PipelineCtx`/`StageHandler`/`StageCache`/`RunOptions`/`PipelineRunResult` types); `pipeline/runPipeline.ts` (`runPipeline`, `summarise`); `pipeline/__tests__/stateMachine.test.ts` (4 spec-named). **Eleven decisions in ADR-062.** (1) Its declared Depends On P-237 was WAVE SEQUENCING, not a code input - P-237 is Web - Perf Pass, and every one of the ten components P-238 composes has been green since P-188, so the phase was takeable; recorded rather than quietly ignored. (2) TRANSITIONS is the single source of truth (the handoff: table-only, no hidden state), because a resumable machine persists only its records and hidden state would be lost on restart. (3) **A REAL BUG THE MANDATED TEST CAUGHT: I first counted failed as SETTLED**, so after a stage failed the executor saw nothing unsettled ahead of it and walked ON, reporting `completed` with a stage sitting failed - it would have silently skipped its own failure; `isSettledStatus` is now `passed` / `skipped` only, since a failed stage is still owed a retry. (4) Resumable BY CONSTRUCTION: the records ARE the cursor, so `run` starts at the first unsettled stage and there is no second resume path to keep in sync. (5) Pausing is first-class: `blocked`/`awaiting_hil` stop the run and report `stoppedAt`, with the return edge in the table. (6) A failed stage is a RESULT, not an err (the P-155 rule); only `ILLEGAL_TRANSITION` (P-159 shape - NO new error code, no ritual) and `INTERNAL` are typed errors. (7) A handler-less stage is a deliberate `skipped`, not a failure. (8) runPipeline is the COMPOSER and the concrete module handlers are INJECTED - wiring P-113/P-131/P-167/P-177/P-187 from here would mean guessing five APIs, and P-192/P-216/P-239 own that choice; documented as a scoped deviation. (9) `StageCache` is a PREDICATE, not a store (P-250 owns persistence). (10) Events are emitted from `transition` itself, so no move can go unobserved. (11) `--jobs`/`--offline` reach handlers and are asserted. 4 tests (all spec-named). Gate: typecheck 0, lint 0, depcruise 0 (186 modules / 482 deps), build 0; core suite 162 passed / 1 skipped files, 2100 passed / 1 skipped tests. |

(2) **Fixtures are committed as CONTENT and materialized at test time** - a literal `.git` inside `src` would be walked by lint/depcruise and a committed object DB bakes in machine-authored SHAs; `commitFiles` + `FIXTURE_IDENTITY` gives shared content AND deterministic authorship, which is what P-204's CLI tests reuse. (3) The chain starts at the STAGED tree + blame; P-072/P-076's merge has its own suite, so C is staged in the P-070 teleport shape P-181 already models. (4) **A real caveat the suite FOUND: `sourceCommit` is the commit C BLAMES, not A's/B's** - a real merge imports upstream objects and this staging does not; asserted as C's own sha (truth) rather than a SHA the fixture cannot produce, recorded as a HARNESS limitation, not a code one. (5) `deterministic map` = two INDEPENDENT passes (fresh blame + fresh map) comparing origins, DTO, `mapChecksum` and CREDITS bytes, plus ADR-059's manifest-hash-across-clocks property. (6) **A second wrong assumption caught: `origins()` sorts by `localeCompare`, NOT default code-unit `.sort()`** - the first version asserted the DTO order equalled `[...files].sort()` and failed on `lib/index.js` vs `README.md`; now asserts the contract that exists. (11) Fixtures are shared with P-204. 42 tests across 8 files under `test:provenance`. | 2026-10-16 | P-187 audit log | **P-187** | The trust record. `provenance/audit.ts` (new): `ProvenanceAudit` (`append`/`query`/`fullAudit`/`exportJson`), `AuditRecord`/`AuditDraft`/`AuditQuery`/`AuditAction`/`ProvenanceAuditOptions`, `sanitizeDetail`, `AUDIT_ACTIONS` (17), `PROVENANCE_AUDIT_TABLE_SQL`; barrels wired. **Eleven decisions in ADR-060.** (1) The `provenance_audit` table is OWNED on first use (the P-145/P-169/P-173/P-178 precedent), NOT a store migration - `store/schema.ts:10` DOES reserve a slot for P-187, so this was a real choice recorded rather than assumed (the phase never asks for the store to be edited, and `DbLike` is `bun:sqlite`-free precisely so an owner can create its own table). (2) It is a **SECOND audit surface**: v1 already has a generic `audit` table and P-145 owns `ai_calls`; neither can answer "what did this stitch do" without wading through unrelated rows. (3) **Append-only is verified STRUCTURALLY** - the test enumerates the class prototype and asserts the ABSENCE of `update`/`delete`/`remove`/`edit`/`clear`/`reset`/`truncate`; a test that merely counted rows after `append` would pass for a class that could also delete them. (4) `detail` is sanitized by **WALKING the object** and scrubbing every string leaf through P-179's `scrubSecrets` - stringifying the whole detail would collapse the structure a caller reads back; non-strings keep their types. (5) **Sanitization happens BEFORE the insert**, and the test queries the RAW table to prove the credential never reaches the row (the P-173 ingress choice, for a different stream). (6) **A durable `DbLike` is REQUIRED, with no in-memory fallback** - an audit that evaporates with the process is not an audit. (7) `id`+`ts` are assigned by `append` and returned; `ts` comes from an INJECTED clock. (8) `exportJson` is ordered OLDEST-first (ts then id) so a compliance dump reads as a story AND is diffable - two exports are byte-identical, and an empty log is `[]`. (9) Filters combine with AND; no match is an empty list, not an error. (10) No new error code. (11) **`orchestration/pipeline.ts` NOT touched** - the phase marks it "later hook" and the pipeline is P-238; `append` is the seam it and P-160's HIL flow call, and the test proves both pipeline actions and the HIL `approve`/`reject` pair record against one `jobId`. 6 tests (all spec-named) against REAL temp SQLite, **passed on the FIRST run**. | 2026-10-16 | P-186 checksums | **P-186** | The tamper-evidence anchor. `provenance/checksums.ts` (new): `buildChecksumManifest`, `verifyChecksums`, `renderChecksumFile`, `ChecksumEntry`/`ChecksumManifest`/`ChecksumVerification`/`BuildChecksumOptions`, `CHECKSUM_MANIFEST_FILENAME`, `CHECKSUM_ALGORITHM`, `DEFAULT_HASH_CONCURRENCY` (8), `DEFAULT_CHECKSUM_IGNORES`; `map.ts` gains ONE ADDITIVE optional field (`checksumManifestPath`, a 3rd ctor param + getter threaded through `from`); barrels wired. **Ten decisions in ADR-059.** (1) **`manifestSha256` EXCLUDES `generatedAt`** - otherwise the hash moves on every rebuild of an UNCHANGED tree, defeating the very stability criterion the phase demands; asserted directly (same tree, different clock, same hash). (4) **An ABSENT file is a MISMATCH, not an error** (`valid:false` for drift AND absence - both mean "C is not what was recorded"); only an unreadable-but-present file is an `err`. (7) Defaults exclude vcs/deps/build output AND the manifest itself, so a second run does not hash its own output. (8) Paths are RELATIVE and posix - a Windows-built manifest must verify on Linux, which is exactly when it matters. (9) The `map.ts` change is ADDITIVE only: existing `from(origins)`/`from(origins, warnings)` calls are untouched, and sqlite load simply has no manifest path (it is a SESSION reference, not persisted). (4) `safeEnv` keeps the mandated `Record<string,string>` return while `safeEnvPlan` carries what `mask` needs (a bare record cannot express "inject the value, mask it in logs"). (5) `allow-list` with no list passes NOTHING (an absent allow-list must not be a full bypass). (6) **`scrubSecrets`'s pass ORDER is load-bearing and the phase's own test caught a REAL LEAK in my first version**: running the key/value pass before the shape pass turned `Authorization: Bearer abcdefgh1234` into `Authorization[REDACTED] abcdefgh1234` â€” scheme word redacted, TOKEN LEFT IN THE TEXT; the shape pass now runs first and pass 3 folds an optional `Bearer/Basic/Digest/Token` prefix so it cannot recur. (7) P-145's `redactAuditText` is REUSED for shapes rather than copying `SECRET_PATTERNS`, and `bun run lint:deps` was RUN to prove no cycle (**0 violations, 171 modules, 463 dependencies**). (11) Content scanning is opt-in AND bounded (64 KiB prefix) â€” a test proves a secret past `maxBytes` is not found. (12) A warning's `excerpt` is MASKED, so a warning can never become the leak it reports. (13) Both new config keys are OPTIONAL nested keys (top-level pinned `jsonSchema` expectations untouched). (14) The client never reads `process.env` and copies rather than mutates the caller's spec. 11 tests (6 spec-named). | 2026-10-16 | P-178 layer cache | **P-178** | Repeat runs skip the pull + reinstall. `sandbox/cache.ts` (new): `cacheKey`, `class SandboxCache` (`get`/`put`/`entries`/`clear`), `restoreCache`, `saveCache`, `clearCacheForTests`, `SANDBOX_CACHE_TABLE_SQL`, `CACHE_KEY_VERSION`, `ECOSYSTEM_MANIFESTS`/`ECOSYSTEM_LOCKFILES`, `FULL_TREE_IGNORES`; `sandbox/install.ts` consults the cache (hit â†’ no container at all) and records the image tag on a miss; `sandbox/runner.ts` resolves the run image from a cache hit when the caller named none; new optional config `sandbox.cacheKeyFullTree`; `sandbox/index.ts` + public-barrel wiring. **Fourteen decisions in ADR-051.** (1) **P-186's "checksum helper" does not exist** â€” grep found only forward references (`git/blameMap.ts:2`, `git/binary.ts:3`), so importing it would have been a phantom API; hashing is local (`node:crypto`), like `license/generate.ts`'s private sha256. (2) `get`/`put` return **`Result`**, not the sketch's bare promises â€” the caller must tell "no entry" from "store failed". (3) **`includeFiles: true` is REFUSED (`CONFIG_ERROR`) when `cacheKeyFullTree` is off** â€” a silently downgraded key is a stale-hit correctness bug, not a cost saving. (4) **`FULL_TREE_IGNORES` is load-bearing**: including `node_modules`/`target`/`dist` would mean the install changes the key that describes the install's input â†’ the cache would MISS FOREVER; `it('ignores install output in a full-tree key')` pins it. (5) The lockfile is IN by default (`includeLock:false` opts out), and a legitimately absent file contributes a stable `-` marker rather than an error. (6) Key is `v1`-prefixed and name-sorted â†’ order-independent, and a format change invalidates everything at once. (7) `kind` = ecosystem; `created_at` from an INJECTED clock. (8) **No new `StitchError` code** (`CONFIG_ERROR`/`INTERNAL` suffice) â€” second consecutive phase to skip the six-edit ritual. (9) **A broken cache never fails a run**: `read-failed` â†’ miss, `write-failed` â†’ install still `ok`; proved with a `DbLike` whose `query` throws. (10) `install.ts` hashes the workspace **once** (`get`'s key is reused by `put`). (12) `clear()` is programmatic; `sandbox cache clear` is P-190's CLI (does not exist) â€” the P-169/P-173 arrangement. | 2026-10-16 | P-177 cleanup | **P-177** | Closes the Sandbox epic with deterministic cleanup on every exit path. `sandbox/cleanup.ts` (new): `cleanupSandbox`, `CleanupTracker` (`track`/`entries`/`size`/`clear`), `withCleanup`, `ResourceKind`/`TrackedResource`/`CleanupSummary`/`CleanupClient`/`CleanupOptions`; `sandbox/client.ts` now exposes `removeImage`/`removeVolume`/`removeNetwork`/`pruneImages` (+ `DockerLike` handles `getImage`/`getVolume`/`getNetwork`/`pruneImages`); `sandbox/runner.ts` wraps the whole dispatch â€” P-176 repeats included â€” in `withCleanup` when a tracker is supplied; new optional config `sandbox.pruneImages`; `sandbox/index.ts` + public-barrel wiring. **Ten decisions in ADR-050.** (1) **`cleanup` returns a `CleanupSummary`, not the sketch's `Promise<void[]>`** â€” the acceptance needs what was removed, what FAILED (as warnings), what was deliberately PRESERVED (`preservedImages`), and an opt-in prune count; a `void[]` has nowhere to put "non-ephemeral preserved". (2) **The TRACKER decides ephemerality**, so "prune only the `stitch-ephemeral-` tag" is `isEphemeralTag(id)` over owned resources â€” no `listImages()` round-trip â€” and a pulled pinned base is reported preserved rather than deleted. (3) **`track` is idempotent per `(kind,id)`** â€” a retry registering the same container must not produce a second, doomed removal reported as noise. (4) **The P-168 client is EXTENDED** (Files list named only `cleanup.ts`/`runner.ts`, but the acceptance cannot be met with `rm` alone), following P-169's `buildImage` and P-171's `copyFrom` precedent; dockerode's `getImage`/`getVolume`/`getNetwork`/`pruneImages` were verified in `@types/dockerode@4.0.1` FIRST, and the widened seam forced three existing test fakes to add four members. (5) **`pruneImages` is opt-in, default `false`** (a daemon-wide prune nukes images repo-stitcher never made), added as a nested key so the pinned `jsonSchema` top-level expectations are untouched. (6) **No new `StitchError` code was needed** â€” `DOCKER_ERROR`/`CONFIG_ERROR` cover removals and `withCleanup` maps a THROWING body to `INTERNAL`, so the six-edit ritual of P-169/P-170/P-174 does not apply here. (7) **A missing client capability is reported, never pretended away** (`errors` + message), so a sweep can never report a false clean. (8) **Order: containers â†’ images â†’ volumes â†’ networks â†’ temp dirs** (containers pin the volumes). (9) **The runner tracker is OPTIONAL** so the P-176 path is byte-for-byte unchanged without one, and `tempDir` removal stays scoped to what a caller tracked â€” P-171's `artifactTarget`/`buildoutDir` are CALLER-owned and destroying them would delete the caller's artifacts. (10) **The summary sink is an injected `onSummary`** because neither P-145's audit log (AI-specific) nor P-248 exists yet. 6 spec-named tests + 3 extras (idempotent tracking, opt-in prune, limited/no client) â†’ 9; the `finally` case drives the REAL `runVerification`. Gate: typecheck 0, lint 0, build 0; full suite 172 files / 2094 tests passed. |
| 2026-10-16 | P-176 verdict + flaky | **P-176** | Turns a raw run into a VERDICT with a bounded repeat check, so a flaky test cannot make the agent loop forever on noise or let the pipeline ship a broken child. `sandbox/verdict.ts` (new): `Verdict`/`VerdictAttempt`/`FlakyThreshold`/`CheckFlakyOptions`/`AttemptDetail`/`RunOutcome`/`FlakyRunner`, `assessVerdict`, `checkFlaky`, `flakySameFileSeen`, `logsDiffer`, `buildWarnings`, `resolveRepeat`, `MAX_FLAKY_REPEAT` (3), `DEFAULT_FLAKY_REPEAT` (2); `sandbox/runner.ts` now calls `checkFlaky` and returns `VerifiedRun` (`(BuildResult\|TestResult) & { verdict? }`); new config `sandbox.flakyCheck` (optional, OFF by default); `sandbox/index.ts` + public-barrel wiring. **Seven decisions in ADR-049.** (1) **`checkFlaky` takes a re-run THUNK, not the spec's `client`** â€” re-running means re-dispatching through P-175 (Docker *or* GitHub, chosen at call time) and the mandated tests are a "fake runner with scripted outcomes"; the thunk keeps one code path and needs no daemon. (2) **Off means off: only `enabled === true` enables repeats** â€” my first version treated `enabled === false` as the only off-switch, so an UNSET config ran three attempts, a real cost bug on the GitHub path (minutes per attempt) that the `disabled by default` test caught. (3) **`pass` comes from the FIRST attempt** â€” acceptance states it exactly ("failing base that passes on retry â†’ `flaky:true, pass:false`"); a green base gets ONE confirmation, a red base gets the caller's budget, and declaring success on a retry would hide the failure it just demonstrated. (4) **`assessVerdict` accepts a single run OR a sequence (additive)**, so the multi-attempt rule has ONE home, with three precise `stableSince` meanings (`first` = all passed, `last` = only the last did, `none` = the last failed). (5) **The Verdict rides via an intersection type**, not by mutating the FROZEN `BuildResult`/`TestResult` shapes (P-171 froze them for web P-220). (6) **`repeat` is clamped to 0..3** (default 2) in `resolveRepeat`, per the handoff's "keep repeat small" and P-175's minutes-per-attempt warning; `repeat: 99` yields 3. (7) **Warnings live on the Verdict** â€” `flakySameFileSeen` names files that failed in SOME attempts (failing in all is broken, not flaky) and a line diff flags a likely timing dependence. 6 tests (all 6 spec-named: passes cleanly / flags flaky on flip / retries failing / bounds repeats / disabled by default / warns on flaky file). Gate: typecheck 0, lint 0, build 0; full suite **171 files / 2088 tests passed**. |
| 2026-10-16 | P-175 GH fallback | **P-175** | Verification that works without Docker. `sandbox/fallback.ts` (new): `fallbackSelection`, `renderWorkflow`, `runViaGitHubActions`, `DEFAULT_WORKFLOW_FILE`, `WORKFLOW_PLACEHOLDERS`, `BackendChoice`/`SelectionSource`/`WorkflowValues`/`GitHubFallbackOptions`; `sandbox/runner.ts` (new): `runVerification` dispatcher + `VerificationKind`/`RunVerificationOptions`; `templates/github-build.yml` (new, the committed workflow); `sandbox/index.ts` + public-barrel wiring; new config `sandbox.mode` (optional enum) and a new optional top-level `ci.path` block. **Seven decisions in ADR-048.** (1) **`sandbox.mode` is a NEW optional field â€” P-009's `backend` is not widened:** the spec wants `'auto'\|'docker'\|'github'` but `SandboxBackendSchema` is `'docker'\|'github-actions'` with no `auto`, and adding a member would force every existing consumer's `switch` to change; the selector prefers `mode` and falls back to `backend`, so precedence is explicit rather than a silent reinterpretation. (2) **`config.ci.path` is a new OPTIONAL top-level `ci` block**, and its presence (+ resolved auth) IS the GH path's flag (step 4). It is the only new top-level key, so `jsonSchema.test.ts`'s pinned `required` list was untouched â€” but its pinned `properties` list broke and was updated, which is the test doing its job. (3) **The template is a committed FILE and rendering takes its TEXT** â€” reading it at runtime breaks in a bundled `dist` (ADR-042's packaging problem) and duplicating it as a constant drifts, so `renderWorkflow(template, values)` is pure and the caller supplies what it read; substitution is strict (a missing value is a typed `CONFIG_ERROR`), like P-141's renderer. **The test caught a real defect in my own template**: the first draft wrapped placeholders in `${{ 'â€¦' }}`, which survived into the rendered output â€” fixed rather than asserted around. (4) **A forced `docker` with no daemon FALLS BACK to a usable GH path and is `unavailable` only when there is none** â€” step 1 says "error if daemon down" but the acceptance criterion refines it ("error only when no GH path"), and refusing to verify when a working backend exists would be the wrong failure. (5) **An unfinished GH run is `SANDBOX_TIMEOUT`, never a verdict** â€” no fabricated pass/fail can reach the loop or the pipeline; latency is documented as minutes-not-seconds. (6) **The GH test path REUSES P-172's `parseTestOutput`**, so "identical shape" is enforced by construction, not convention. (7) **`runVerification` separates "no backend exists" (`SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE`, an environment state) from "the wiring is missing" (`CONFIG_ERROR`, a caller bug)** â€” asserted both ways, because collapsing them sends a user to fix their machine when the bug is in the call. 6 tests across `fallback.test.ts` (selects backend / runs via github actions / parses gh logs to BuildResult / gates on auth) + `runner.test.ts` (errors when unavailable / same shape both paths), the last asserting the shared field set for BOTH build and test. | 2026-10-16 | P-174 timeout/limits | **P-174** | Hard limits so a runaway merged repo cannot hang or starve the host. `sandbox/limits.ts` (new): `LimitsConfig`, `DEFAULT_LIMITS`, `STRICT_LIMITS`, `applyLimits`, `enforceTimeout`, `defaultLimits`, `limitsToCaptureOptions`, `parseMemoryToMb`, `parseCpuCount`, `LimitsConfigSource`; `sandbox/index.ts` + public-barrel wiring; P-168's `run` adopts the new stop codes. **Six decisions in ADR-047, led by the handoff's own goal** ("expose failures distinctly (timeout vs OOM vs abort) so P-163 can route them"). (1) **`SANDBOX_TIMEOUT` (56) and `SANDBOX_ABORTED` (57) are new codes and P-168's `run` ADOPTS them** â€” it previously reported a wall-clock overrun as `DOCKER_ERROR` and an abort as the generic P-140 `ABORTED`, both wrong for routing; one vocabulary now holds whichever layer catches the stop, and `enforceTimeout` reports the same pair. (2) **`enforceTimeout(fn, timeoutMs, signal)` owns a clock; container cleanup stays P-168's** â€” the phase says "on timeout `client.kill(id)` + `client.rm(id)`", but `run` removes its container in a `finally` and never exposes a handle, so a wrapper holding an id could not be generic and a second killer would race the first; the container really being gone is asserted END-TO-END (a real `SandboxClient` over a fake docker, `removed === ['t1']`), not assumed. (3) **Limits parse Docker's own notation** (`2g`/`512m`/`1.5g`/`1024k`/bare-MB â†’ MB; `cpu` â†’ cores incl. fractions; `timeout` is SECONDS), and an unreadable value is a typed `CONFIG_ERROR`, never a silent `0` that would drop the protection â€” so `defaultLimits` returns a `Result` (the spec sketched a bare return). (4) **The spec's listed defaults LOSE to the config's actual defaults:** step 1 lists `300_000ms/1536MB/2` but labels them "config P-009", and P-009's real schema is `memory:'2g', cpu:'1', timeout:600` â€” i.e. 2048 MB / 1 core / 600 s; the acceptance says "Defaults from config", so config wins (600 s â†’ `600_000` ms, documented) and the step-1 numbers survive only as the no-config fallback. (5) **`OomKillDisable` is deliberately NOT set** â€” the Files list says "client.ts (optionally set OOM)", but disabling the OOM killer would let a runaway allocation starve the host, the exact failure class this phase prevents; a test asserts the field is absent so it cannot drift. (6) **`strictLimits()` tightens only time/memory/CPU/network** (log/artifact bounds stay P-173's constants because they protect host memory, not the code under test), wired by `limitsToCaptureOptions`. 7 tests (6 spec-named: applies memory/cpu/network / kills on timeout / aborts on signal / strict limits / defaults read config / bounds output). Gate: typecheck 0, lint 0, build 0; full suite **168 files / 2075 tests passed**. |
| 2026-10-15 | P-173 capture | **P-173** | Makes a failed stitch reproducible post-hoc: bounded logs + run-scoped artifacts + persisted metadata. `sandbox/capture.ts` (new): `CaptureManager`, `bindRun`/`append`/`logs`/`entries`/`dropped`/`capture`/`extractArtifacts`/`getRun`/`clear`, `hostArtifactPath`, `sanitizeSegment`, `LogEntry`/`LogSink`/`CaptureResult`/`CaptureRunRecord`/`CaptureOptions`/`CaptureClient`, `DEFAULT_LOG_CAPACITY` (10_000), `DEFAULT_MAX_ARTIFACT_BYTES` (100 MB), `CAPTURE_RUNS_TABLE_SQL`; `sandbox/client.ts` gains `logs()` (demultiplexed), `followLogs()` (raw stream) and `RunOptions.onLog`; `sandbox/index.ts` + public-barrel wiring; new config `sandbox.maxArtifactBytes`. **Six decisions in ADR-046.** (1) **`capture()` returns a `Result` and overflow is `err(SANDBOX_LOG_LIMIT)`** â€” step 1 sketches a bare `Promise<{logs,sizeBytes}>` and then says "raise SANDBOX_LOG_LIMIT", which a no-error-channel method cannot do; the parenthetical "(but keep latest)" says the operation PARTLY succeeded, so the typed err carries `dropped`/`retained` while the lines stay retrievable via `logs(runId)` (and `SANDBOX_LOG_LIMIT`'s hint is classified `continue`, not `abort`). (2) **Timestamps live in the buffer, not the spec's shape:** the handoff wants ISO timestamps per line for re-run diffing, so entries are `{ts,line}` and `entries(runId)` exposes them while `logs(runId)` keeps the spec's `string[]`; overflow keeps the NEWEST lines ("bounded memory, not truncation"). (5) **Two new codes, both carrying `message`** â€” `SANDBOX_LOG_LIMIT` (54) and `SANDBOX_ARTIFACT_LIMIT` (55), six coordinated edits each; the `message` is deliberate because P-170 proved a code without one breaks the cli's `describeError` default branch. (6) **Artifact safety is a pure, tested gate** â€” `sanitizeSegment` refuses separators/`..`/`.`/drive prefixes/NUL for BOTH the run id and each name, `extractArtifacts` refuses a traversing run id before copying (asserted with zero copy calls), the cumulative written size is bounded by `sandbox.maxArtifactBytes`, and the run dir is built by a separator-aware join rather than the string slicing the first draft used. 5 tests (all 5 spec-named: streams into bounded buffer / caps at log limit / extracts artifacts / sanitizes artifact paths / binds onLog to run). | 2026-10-15 | P-172 run tests | **P-172** | The structured test verdict that gates finalising C and feeds P-176. `sandbox/test.ts` (new): `runTests`, `TestResult`/`TestFailure`/`RunTestsOptions`/`TestClient`/`ParsedTestCounts`, `TEST_COMMANDS`, `parseTestOutput`, `parseJunitXml`, `looksLikeJunit`, `DEFAULT_TEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, `MAX_TEST_FAILURES`; `sandbox/index.ts` + public-barrel wiring. **Six decisions in ADR-045, led by a measurement.** Step 2 asks for a "JUNIT/json reporter flag where supported â€¦ else parse stdout regex" â€” **and no runner can put a machine-readable report on stdout:** `bun test --reporter=junit` REQUIRES `--reporter-outfile` (verified directly via `bun test --help` â€” it writes a FILE), `pytest --junit-xml` writes a file, `cargo test --format json` needs nightly, and `go test -json` exists but go is not installed here so it could not be verified. (2) A real **JUnit parser IS implemented** and honoured when the output is XML; nothing emits that today, but P-173's artifact capture lifts reporter files out of the container, so the support the handoff asks to keep documented is real code, not a promise â€” with a text fallback for malformed XML rather than zeros. | 2026-10-15 | P-171 run build | **P-171** | The central verification, whose `BuildResult` is FROZEN (web P-220 + CLI P-199 render it). `sandbox/build.ts` (new): `runBuild`, `BuildResult`, `RunBuildOptions`, `BuildClient`, `BUILD_COMMANDS`, `DEFAULT_OUTPUT_DIRS`, `boundLogLines`, `MAX_BUILD_LOG_LINES`, `DEFAULT_BUILD_TIMEOUT_MS`, `WORKSPACE_MOUNT` (re-exported from P-170), `BUILDOUT_MOUNT`; `SandboxClient.copyFrom` + `RunOptions.capture` + `ContainerRun.capturedArchive` + `ContainerLike.getArchive` added to the P-168 client; `sandbox/index.ts` + public-barrel wiring. **Seven decisions in ADR-044.** (1) **A structural conflict: the artifact copy could not happen where the spec put it.** Step 2 says "on completion â€¦ `client.copyFrom(id, â€¦)`", but P-168's `run` removes its container in a `finally`, so nothing is left to copy from afterwards (and keeping it alive would break P-168's cleanup acceptance criterion). | 2026-10-15 | P-170 install deps | **P-170** | The step between resolving an image (P-169) and building (P-171). `sandbox/install.ts` (new): `installDeps`, `InstallSpec`/`InstallResult`/`InstallOptions`/`InstallClient`, `countInstalled`, `WORKSPACE_MOUNT`, `DEFAULT_INSTALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, `NODE_LOCKFILES`; `sandbox/index.ts` + public-barrel wiring. **Seven decisions in ADR-043.** (1) **A cross-phase inconsistency, caught before it bit:** P-169's step-1 note says "For npm use the bun-ready node image", but the value it committed was `node:22-alpine` â€” which cannot work, because P-170's node install is `bun install` and P-155's build/test are `bun run build`/`bun test`. | 2026-10-15 | P-169 sandbox images | **P-169** | Pinned per-ecosystem images with a cached, deterministic resolution. `sandbox/images.ts` (new): `ECOSYSTEM_IMAGES`, `imageFor`, `isEphemeralTag`, `ephemeralTagFor`, `EPHEMERAL_PREFIX`, `SANDBOX_IMAGES_TABLE_SQL`, `ensureImage`, `clearImageCacheForTests`, `ImageClient`/`EnsureImageOptions`; `sandbox/Dockerfile.node|python|rust|go` (new, P-008 base extended); `SandboxClient.buildImage` + a shared `#drainStream` added to `sandbox/client.ts`; `sandbox/index.ts` + public-barrel wiring. **Six decisions in ADR-042.** (1) **The spec's registry keys are wrong for this codebase:** step 1 sketches `{ npm, python, cargo, go }`, but the real `Ecosystem` union is `node|python|rust|go` (`npm`/`cargo` are the *parser* ids), and every consumer â€” P-155's `BuildEcosystem`/`ECOSYSTEM_COMMANDS`, P-103's `detectEcosystems` â€” speaks the latter, so an `npm`-keyed map would be unusable from `run_build`. The Dockerfiles are therefore `Dockerfile.node|python|rust|go`: step 2's lookup is `Dockerfile.<eco>` keyed on the ecosystem, so `Dockerfile.npm`/`.cargo` could never be found (dead files). (2) **`DOCKER_BUILD_FAILED` did not exist** â†’ code **51**, six coordinated edits (union, `STITCH_ERROR_CODES`, `doctor.ts`, P-163's `Record<StitchErrorCode, ErrorRule>` â€” the compile error fired again â€” `build.smoke` 50â†’51, `INTEGRATIONS.md` Â§9.1). (3) **`config.sandbox.overrideImage`/`buildLocal` did not exist** â†’ added as **optional** (the P-168 precedent; no `defaultConfig` churn). (4) **`client.buildImage` did not exist on the P-168 client** â†’ added, dockerode-shaped, draining the build stream (a `{"error":â€¦}` line is a failure even when the call resolved) and mapping to `DOCKER_BUILD_FAILED`; the drain was factored into one `#drainStream`. The build CONTEXT is a tar path/stream passed through unchanged â€” **no `tar` dependency is added** â€” so `buildLocal` requires an explicit `dockerfileDir` (context defaults to it). (5) The `ensureImage` seam is the narrow structural `ImageClient` because `SandboxClient` has `#private` fields and is therefore nominally typed (no fake assignable) â€” the ADR-030 rule. (6) The `sandbox_images` cache table is owned and created on first use (the P-145/P-160 pattern), keyed `ecosystem\|tag\|platform` with an upsert, platform recorded (`null` when unqualified); a missing `dockerfileDir`/Dockerfile under `buildLocal` is a typed `CONFIG_ERROR`, never a silent pull. 10 tests (6 spec-named: defaults images per ecosystem / overrides from config / pulls when not cached / builds local dockerfile / caches across calls / maps pull failure) + the client's new `builds an image`. | 2026-10-15 | P-168 Docker client | **P-168** | Epic 8 opens with the container-lifecycle wrapper the whole Sandbox epic builds on. `sandbox/client.ts` (new): `class SandboxClient` + `ContainerSpec`/`ContainerRun`/`SandboxStats`, `demuxDockerLogs`, `adaptDocker`/`adaptContainer`, `MAX_CONTAINER_LOG_LINES = 500`, `DEFAULT_RUN_TIMEOUT_MS`; `isAvailable()` (delegates to the P-067 `pingDocker`, false-never-throw), `pull()` (drains the progress stream, `<code>{"error":...}</code>` line OR rejection â†’ `DOCKER_PULL_FAILED`), `run(spec, {timeoutMs, signal})` (createâ†’startâ†’waitâ†’logsâ†’remove in a `finally`), `rm()` (best-effort force-remove, never throws), `stats()` (CPU%/memory), `SandboxClient.create()`; `sandbox/index.ts` (new barrel) + public-barrel wiring. **Six decisions in ADR-041.** (1) **`DOCKER_PULL_FAILED` did not exist** â€” the spec mandates it, and it is genuinely distinct from `DOCKER_ERROR` (pull = image/registry/auth, and it is what P-175 keys its GH-Actions fallback off). Added as **code 50** with the SAME six coordinated edits P-164 used for `TOKEN_LIMIT`: union, `STITCH_ERROR_CODES`, `doctor.ts`'s exhaustive renderer, **P-163's `Record<StitchErrorCode, ErrorRule>` in `agent/errors.ts`** (the compile error fired exactly as designed), `build.smoke` 49â†’50, and `INTEGRATIONS.md` Â§9.1. (2) **`config.sandbox.networkAllow` did not exist** â€” added as an **optional** boolean so `defaultConfig` is untouched and existing configs are unchanged (`undefined` = allowed, the pre-P-168 behaviour); the client takes it as an option (testable without a `Config`) and the orchestrator passes `config.sandbox.networkAllow`; P-174 owns the product default. (3) The client **wraps** the existing P-067 surface (`createDockerClient`, `pingDocker`, `defaultDockerSocket`) rather than reimplementing reachability, so there is one policy in the codebase. (4) **The dockerode seam is a narrow structural interface plus explicit `adaptDocker`/`adaptContainer` adapters, with NO cast** â€” `Docker.createContainer` and `Container.wait`/`logs`/`remove`/`stats` are overload sets (`(options, callback)` + `(options)`), and TS checks a single-signature target against the FIRST overload only, so assigning a real `Docker` fails `Expected 2 or more arguments, but got 1`; naming each member explicitly sidesteps it. (Measured, not guessed: the identical error recurred on `Container.wait` after `createContainer` was fixed.) (5) Timeout â†’ `DOCKER_ERROR` (carrying `containerId`), abort â†’ `ABORTED`; only the spec-named pull failure needed a new code. Tests narrow on `.code` before reading `.message` because a union member (`SANDBOX_FAILED`) has no `message` â€” the P-124 lesson. | 2026-09-26 | P-164 loop cap | **P-164** | The phase that makes the agent's central promise - spend is bounded - actually true. `agent/cap.ts` (new): `LoopCap`, `LoopContext`, `CapStop`, `LoopCapGuard` (`check`/`canRaise`/`raise`), `resolveCap`, `readCapConfig`, `DEFAULT_MAX_ITERATIONS = 20`; `[agent]` config gains `maxIterations` (20) / `maxTokens` (0=off) / `maxCostUsd` (0=off); `ai/types.ts` gains the `LoopCapLike` seam and `LoopConfig.cap`; `ai/loop.ts` makes the guard the loop's admission gate. **New `StitchError` code 49 `TOKEN_LIMIT`** (four coordinated edits: union, `STITCH_ERROR_CODES`, `doctor.ts`, `build.smoke.test.ts` 48 -> 49). Seven spec conflicts plus one defect the tests caught in my own code, all in **ADR-037**. (1) The Files list names `agent/loop.ts` (line 8294) - the **seventh** consecutive Epic 7 phase with the same wrong path. (2) **The spec names an error code that does not exist**: step 2 AND acceptance criterion 2 both require `err('TOKEN_LIMIT')`, but `TOKEN_LIMIT` was absent from the union, absent from the root barrel, and the only `*LIMIT*` codes were `COST_LIMIT`/`RATE_LIMIT`/`LOOP_LIMIT`. Tokens and dollars are deliberately NOT collapsed - a cheap model can exhaust a token cap with no dollar consequence and an expensive one can hit dollars first, so reporting one as the other sends an operator to the wrong setting. (3) **The spec's comparison cannot compile**: `ctx.costTracker.get totalTokens > maxTokens`, but `CostTracker.totalTokens` is a `TokenUsage` **object**, not a number. (4) `ctx: LoopContext` names a type that does not exist, and `ToolContext.config` is `Record<string, unknown>` rather than `Config`, so reading `[agent]` is a narrowing job - done once in `readCapConfig` with no casts. (5) **The iteration rule is off by one**: "consult at the top of each iteration" plus `iterations > maxIterations` permits `maxIterations + 1` calls, so the guard refuses on `>=` and the loop's existing tested behaviour is preserved - `loop.test.ts` passes unchanged, which is the evidence. **THE TRAP THIS PHASE HAD TO AVOID: A GUARD THAT NEVER FIRES.** The loop was already `for (iterations = 0; iterations < maxIterations; ...)` and already returned `LOOP_LIMIT`, so layering a guard on top meant the **bound fired first** and the guard was dead code that only ran in unit tests - a spend guard that is never consulted. The bound was removed, the loop became `for (;;)`, and TypeScript then proved the old fall-through `return err({code:'LOOP_LIMIT'})` unreachable, so it was **deleted** rather than left as a second answer to the same question. **A CONFIGURED DOLLAR BUDGET WITH NO PRICED TRACKER IS AN ERROR, NOT A NO-OP**: the loop's own `ModelSpec` is a placeholder with `cost: { input: 0, output: 0 }`, so real pricing exists only in an injected `CostTracker`, and silently not enforcing a spend cap is exactly the failure this phase exists to prevent - it returns `CONFIG_ERROR` on `agent.maxCostUsd` instead. The **token** budget needs no tracker, because the loop accumulates `usage.total` itself, so it is enforceable for every caller. **`0` MEANS OFF, decided in exactly one place** (`budgetOf`): the config defaults are `0`, and read literally as limits **every default run would stop on its first iteration**. The cap is checked **first in the iteration body**, before the P-159 state moves, because transitioning to `streaming` and then refusing would publish a P-162 `state` event for an iteration that never ran - the exact "it never happened" vs "it failed" confusion P-162 exists to prevent; a test asserts exactly `maxIterations` `streaming` events. Cap stops emit `{type:'complete', finishReason:'length'}` per the handoff - `length` being the honest existing `FinishReason` - so P-162's union contract is unchanged. `COST_LIMIT` gained an **optional** `message` (backward compatible; `doctor.test.ts` already constructs one and still typechecks) so a cap stop can carry a reason like the other two. `ai/loop.ts` imports `agent/cap.ts` as the **one exception to the eight ADR-030 seams**, because those exist to break a cycle and `cap.ts` imports nothing from `ai/` - and the spec puts the guard's construction in the loop, so injecting it would have been more improvisation than importing it. **A REAL DEFECT THE TESTS CAUGHT, AND IT WAS MINE: `autoRaiseBudget` raised the cap without bound, and the test HUNG.** Not a flaky test - a genuine design fault: a human who keeps saying yes is answered, the budget doubles, the loop resumes, hits the new ceiling, asks again, and runs forever, converting "spend is bounded" into unbounded spend with extra steps. Fixed with `maxRaises` (default 1), enforced **inside the guard** so it holds however a caller reaches it; one raise is the useful case (the cap was set too low for a job that is nearly done). Two follow-on defects the same test exposed: the loop **rebuilt the guard** after a raise, resetting the counter and making the bound unreachable (`raise` now mutates in place, so counter and cap cannot disagree), and `raise` **doubled the stop's stale limit** so the second raise returned 4 again - a silent no-op that still consumed the allowance, reading as "approved and nothing happened". **P-163's machinery caught the new code twice, exactly as designed**: its `Record<StitchErrorCode, ErrorRule>` forced the `TOKEN_LIMIT` rule to be written (compile error otherwise) and its hardcoded non-recoverable list failed until `TOKEN_LIMIT` was added. Six spec-named tests plus off-by-one, `0`=off, unenforceable-budget, narrowing, and seam-assignability coverage. |
| 2026-09-26 | P-163 error handling | **P-163** | The phase that gives every error a name, a class, a recovery, and a sentence a user can act on. `agent/errors.ts` (new): `ErrorClass` (the spec-verbatim 8-member union, frozen as the contract P-201/P-228 render from), `classifyError`, `handleError`, `friendlyError`, `fromUnknownThrow`, and an explicit `Record<StitchErrorCode, ErrorRule>` over **all 48 codes**; `ai/types.ts` gains the `AgentErrorRouter` seam (seventh use of the ADR-030 pattern) and `LoopConfig.errorRouter`; `ai/loop.ts` wraps the single `tool.execute()` call in a catch-all. Six spec conflicts plus one design substitution, all in **ADR-036**. (1) The Files list names `agent/loop.ts` (line 8235) - the **sixth** consecutive Epic 7 phase with the same wrong path (P-157:7911, P-158:7968, P-159:8023, P-160:8075, P-162:8180, P-163:8235). (2) **The decisive measurement: the spec's six prefix rules cover 11 of the 48 codes.** Verified by extracting every `code: '...'` from `result/index.ts` - the other **37 would all fall through to `fatal`**, and several demonstrably should not: `RATE_LIMIT` is retryable by definition, `SCANCODE_MISSING` is this repo's own opt-in-not-installed state (P-130), `LOOP_LIMIT`/`NO_FAILOVER`/`USER_CANCELLED` are terminal *outcomes* rather than bugs, and `DOCKER_ERROR` is sandbox work `SANDBOX_*` never matches. "Fatal" means a human must change something, and a rate limit does not qualify. (3) **A prefix is not a category:** `GIT_ERROR.startsWith('GIT')` is true and so is `GITHUB_API_ERROR`, yet a remote API failure and a local repo failure need different recoveries - asserted directly in the tests, because a `startsWith` classifier gets one of them wrong by construction. (4) The spec's "`AUTH_*`/401" conflates two different things: `StitchError` codes `AUTH_ERROR`/`AUTH_FAILED`, *and* `ProviderError.kind === 'auth'`, a different type with a different remedy path. Both are classified, separately. (5) **Step 3 never maps two of the eight classes** - `provider` and `git` are both unmapped. Resolved: `provider` skips straight to failover (the provider already refused once, so retrying the same request is pointless) then aborts; `git` continues, because the spec calls it "recoverable with guidance" and the model is told what git said and can adapt. (6) Step 3's signature `handleError(e, ctx, loop)` drops the `loop` parameter - nothing in the decision calls back into the loop, and taking a whole loop as an argument would make the function untestable in isolation; the recovery decision is a *value* the loop acts on. **The substitution that makes the phase work: an explicit table keyed by `StitchErrorCode`, not prefix rules** - so adding a code to the union without classifying it is a **compile error**, not a review comment, and a test asserts coverage at runtime on top of that. Drift is impossible rather than merely unlikely. Each rule carries class + action + sentence, so **a code cannot have a classification but no message**, which matters because CLI P-201 and web P-228 render from `friendlyError`. `recoverable = cls !== 'fatal' && cls !== 'config'` is spec-verbatim, stated once in `isRecoverable()` so both paths apply one rule - and TypeScript proved the extraction worth it, because the provider branch's class set excludes `'fatal'` entirely, making the inline comparison a **provably-dead comparison the compiler rejected**. `ProviderError` is classified from its own `kind` **and** its own `retryable` flag, because a rate limit the provider says is not retryable must not be retried whatever the kind implies. **The retry -> failover -> abort chain terminates by construction**: budget is an explicit `ErrorBudget` *input*, not a counter buried in the loop, so `handleError` is a pure function of `(error, budget)` and the test drives it to termination without a timer. `ABORTED`/`USER_CANCELLED`/`USER_TIMEOUT`/`LOOP_LIMIT` are `fatal` but their messages never say "bug", because they are outcomes a person chose or a budget reached - enforced by a test. **The loop catch-all IS the acceptance criterion "loop never crashes on unknown error":** a tool is typed to return a `Result`, but one that *throws* - a bug, or a facade that throws before its own guard - would escape `runToolLoop` and take the stitch with it, so the catch wraps the single `execute()` call and turns the throw into an `INTERNAL` tool result plus a `tool_result` reasoning event, and the run continues. It sits there rather than around the whole loop because everything outside that one call already returns a `Result` and cannot throw; the router is optional, and the loop is proven safe with it absent too. **No new `StitchError` code** (48 unchanged) - classification introduces no failure mode. **Two test-expectation bugs of mine, recorded:** I omitted `UNKNOWN_MODEL` from the expected non-recoverable set (the table was right, my list was incomplete), and I asserted `GIT_ERROR` -> `continue` while `handleError` mapped the whole `git` class to `abort` - the test caught a genuine **inconsistency inside my own code** (the table declared `continue`, the switch declared `abort`) and the spec's "recoverable" settled it to `continue`. Six spec-named tests plus a whole-table coverage sweep over all 48 codes. |
| 2026-09-26 | P-162 reasoning stream | **P-162** | The phase that makes the agent legible - every policy verdict, tool call, tool result, state transition, failover and cost tick published live to CLI P-199 and web P-216 over WS. `agent/reasoning.ts` (new): `ReasoningStream` (`emit`/`subscribe`/`dump`, bounded history, isolated subscribers), the 8-variant `ReasoningEvent` union, `reasoningText(ev)`, `MAX_REASONING_HISTORY = 1000`; `ai/types.ts` gains the `ReasoningSink` seam and ai-side `ReasoningEventLike`. Four spec conflicts plus one omitted requirement, all in **ADR-035**. (1) The Files list names `agent/loop.ts` (line 8180) - the **fifth** consecutive Epic 7 phase to do so (P-157:7911, P-158:7968, P-159:8023, P-160:8075, P-162:8180), so the template defect is now unambiguous. (2) The `policy` variant is typed with P-157's `PolicyDecision` - same module, so no cycle and none of the structural duplication P-159's `AgentState`/`AgentStateName` pair needed; the real type is **imported**, not restated. (3) Step 3 says "`plan` when building a prompt (P-141)" but **`buildPrompt` does not exist** - verified: `ai/prompts.ts` exports `render`, `systemPrompt`, `resolveDepsPrompt`, `generateComponentPrompt`, `detectGapsPrompt`, `promptVersion`, none carrying agent intent, and no function by that name anywhere in the repo. So the `plan` event is emitted **once per turn from the text the model actually streamed back** - the honest source, and per-turn rather than per-chunk keeps the feed readable. (4) **The handoff note's "dedupe `state`/`policy` bursts so the UI isn't spammed" directly contradicts the acceptance criterion "No events lost"** - those cannot both hold at `emit`, so dedupe belongs at the **render layer** and `ReasoningStream` publishes everything. **The requirement the spec omits and that matters most: a THROWING SUBSCRIBER MUST NOT BREAK THE AGENT.** A WS bridge with a closed socket, or a serialisation bug in a forwarder, must not fail the stitch it is only observing - every listener call is isolated, the failure is logged and counted in `subscriberFailures`, and peer listeners still receive the event. Tested directly: a throwing subscriber alongside a healthy one on the same emit. **`ReasoningEvent` is NOT `ReasoningChunk`** - verified `ReasoningChunk` is the *provider's* raw stream (`text|tool_call|done|error`) while `ReasoningEvent` is the *agent's* account of itself; the spec's "via P-136" framing blurs the two, and declaring a second copy of `ReasoningChunk` here would have been the mistake. **Live delivery is complete but the post-hoc buffer is bounded** - the two acceptance criteria are reconciled rather than traded: a subscriber attached at emit time receives every event without exception, while `dump()` is capped at 1000, drops the OLDEST (so a review shows the end of the run, where a problem is), and **reports what it dropped** because a silently truncated audit trail is indistinguishable from one that never happened. Same rule as `UndoStack`. **Every exit path publishes a `tool_result`** - unknown tool, arg-rejected (P-158), denied by policy, rejected by a human, blocked by the P-159 lifecycle guard, rejected by validation, and ordinary success/failure. A call that vanished from the feed and a call that failed read very differently, and only the second is honest. `state` events are published from the single `move()` funnel, so each transition appears exactly once and in order, and a **refused** transition publishes nothing - the agent did not go anywhere. Rendered text goes through P-145's `redactAuditText` because this is a user-facing surface, not a redacting log pipeline. **No new `StitchError` code** (48 unchanged) - an observability layer that can fail is not something this phase should introduce. **A real bug the suite caught, mine:** I hardcoded `ok: true` on the success-path `tool_result`, so every tool result looked successful - a failing tool was reported as `ok` with an empty summary. That is precisely the "it never happened" vs "it failed" confusion this phase exists to prevent, and it would have shipped a UI that lied about every failure. Two test bugs fixed at source with the reasoning recorded: my hand-rolled `cost` stand-in accumulated into a different object than the loop read, so it reported zero; and the redaction marker is `[REDACTED]` (uppercase). The cost investigation surfaced a genuine improvement - `estimateCost` divides per-million-token prices by 1e6, so a short run legitimately costs a fraction of a cent, and fixed 4dp rendered that as `$0.0000`, which reads as "free"; `formatCost` now scales precision with magnitude. 5/5 spec-named tests plus ordering, subscriber-isolation and redaction coverage. |
| 2026-09-26 | P-161 revert a tool action | **P-161** | The safety net that makes the `auto` autonomy class acceptable. `agent/revert.ts` (new): `UndoStack` (maxDepth 50), `REVERTERS`, `UNDO_DERIVERS`, `deriveUndoRecord`, `createUndoRecorder`, `revertLastToolAction`, `MAX_UNDO_DEPTH`; `agent/tools/revert_last.ts` (new) with the gated `revert_last` tool; `agent/types.ts` gains `UndoKind`/`UndoRecord`/`FileSnapshot`. Five spec conflicts, all in **ADR-034**. (1) The Files list **omits** `agent/loop.ts` while step 3 says "Wire into the loop" â€” an omission rather than the wrong path that P-157â€“P-160 all had; the loop is still `ai/loop.ts`. (2) Step 2 specifies `undo(): Result<UndoRecord, StitchError>` but also says undo "invoke[s] the matching reverter" â€” and a reverter doing real git work can fail, so returning only the popped record would report success for a failed revert; `undo()` returns the *reverter's* error. (3) Step 4's "Persist the stack in the job store (P-239)" is the **third** forward-reference in three consecutive phases, still unimplemented; the `UndoStack` is injectable so a store-backed one drops in when P-239 lands. (4) Step 2 treats `ctx.git.writeFile`/`move` as guaranteed, but they are **optional** `GitFacade` members (ADR-021) â€” P-154's own `applyProposal` guards `writeFile === undefined` with a typed `CONFIG_ERROR`, and P-161 follows that precedent. (5) `UndoRecord.{before, after}` are `unknown` and **cannot express a `file_move`'s import rewrites** (P-153), which are half the work of undoing a move. **The capability gap the spec did not anticipate:** reverting a `proposal_apply` means *deleting* the files it created (acceptance criterion 3), and there was **no delete capability anywhere on `ToolContext`** â€” verified by grepping `ai/types.ts` for delete/remove/unlink, zero hits. Added `deleteFile?` as an **optional** `GitFacade` member, exactly the ADR-021 pattern: injected, never constructed, absence a typed error. `before` is **`FileSnapshot[]`, not a string**, because `content: null` means *the file did not exist* â€” the case that makes reverting a created file a deletion, and one a plain before-string cannot express. **The single most important decision: a `file_move` revert inverts the rewrites with P-153's `rewriteImportRefs` rather than restoring whole files.** A move rewrites third-party files, so restoring their entire prior content would destroy any *unrelated* edit made to them after the move; a revert that silently destroys work is worse than no revert. Counter-tested: the test edits an importer after the move and asserts that edit survives. **A failed revert KEEPS its record** â€” the tree was not restored, so the history must not claim otherwise, and a record lost to a transient git error is unrecoverable. **The cap drops the OLDEST, not the newest**, and reports what it dropped, because a silently truncated undo history is indistinguishable from "there is nothing to undo". **Every required capability is checked before the first write**, so a revert either restores the whole set or refuses â€” never half-applies, which with the record already popped would leave the tree inconsistent with the history. `revert_last` is **gated**: a revert is itself a mutation, the risk is symmetric, and P-160's queue parks it like any other gate. New code `NOTHING_TO_UNDO` (**48** total) â€” the spec names none for an empty stack and nothing existing means it, because the loop must distinguish "nothing to undo" from "the undo failed". **The decision this phase is really about â€” what the stack does NOT record:** `UNDO_DERIVERS` covers `edit_config` and `move_file` only. `fix_dependency` returns `{action,package,version,section}` and `applyProposal` returns `{written}` â€” **neither carries the prior content of the file it wrote**. Both reverter *kinds* are fully implemented and tested (by pushing records directly), but the two tools do not yet emit records and are deliberately absent from the registry: recording them would put an entry in a stack that advertises reversibility it does not have, and a human told an action is revertable will trust it. Making them emit means a read-before-write in each, changing their contracts and their own tests â€” a named follow-up, not silently absorbed. P-154 already anticipated the split: its `applyProposal` carries the comment *"P-161 owns cross-tool reversion; this applies forward only"*. A real bug the suite caught, mine: the test's inline recorder spread a `null` from `deriveUndoRecord` into `push` and threw before `push` could return its error â€” fixed by moving the null-handling into a proper `createUndoRecorder(stack)` adapter so no caller has to think about it. 6/6 spec-named tests; reverts run against a filesystem-shaped fake tree (not a call-recording mock) so "reverts land as git changes" is a claim about what was written, inspectable and mutable between steps. |

(5) The handoff freezes the nine names for web P-216, making them a public contract. `agent/state.ts` (new): `AgentStateMachine` (`state`/`canTransition`/`canStartTool`/`transition`/`restore`/`history`), `AGENT_STATES`, `LEGAL_TRANSITIONS`, `canTransition`, `isTerminalState`, `isWaitingState`, `canStartToolFrom`, `isAgentState`, `memoryStateStore`, `MAX_STATE_HISTORY = 500`. **`AgentState` and `AgentStateName` are the same type declared twice** â€” two unions of identical string literals are identical to TypeScript, so `ai/loop.ts` drives the lifecycle without importing `agent/state.ts` (ADR-030's cycle-breaker, third use); `state.test.ts` asserts they are assignment-compatible so drift is a test failure, not a silent mismatch. **The HIL guard needed two mechanisms, and the naive one is wrong.** The criterion is "cannot execute a tool while in `waiting_*`". Forbidding `waiting_* â€” tool_executing` would make `ask_user` *unrepresentable* â€” the tool that asked the question is still mid-execution when the answer arrives and must resume. So `canTransition` answers "is this move in the map" (`waiting_user â€” tool_executing` is legal, it is the *return*), while **`canStartTool()` answers "may a tool execution BEGIN now" and is false in both `waiting_*` states**. The loop checks the second right before `execute`, and **the transition itself is the guard**: `move('tool_executing')` returns `ILLEGAL_TRANSITION` when a decision is outstanding, the tool never runs, and the refusal becomes the tool's result â€” one mechanism, no parallel bookkeeping. `waiting_approval` deliberately has **no** direct edge to `tool_executing`; the gate must resolve and hand control back (`waiting_approval â€” iterating`) before anything executes, so the adjacency map does the HIL-pause work. **`waiting_user` is driven by wrapping the responder, not by the tool** â€” the tool layer must not know a state machine exists, so the loop decorates whatever `HumanResponder` it was given: `present()` moves to `waiting_user`, the `finally` moves back to `tool_executing`. **Terminal state is a function of the loop's outcome:** every `ok` return ends in `done` (including a truncated `length`/`content_filter` finish â€” the finishReason rides in the result, and conflating "stopped early" with "failed" would hide a real signal) and every `err` return in `error`, including abort and loop-limit. That is what makes "fatal error â€” `error`, not stuck `iterating`" structural: no exit path leaves a job parked in a non-terminal state, indistinguishable from a running one. **A refused move is inert** â€” no state change, no history entry, and no `onChange` (subscribers are told what happened, not what was attempted). **A failing store does not undo a transition:** the agent genuinely is in that state, and faking otherwise would desynchronise the machine from the loop that drove it; a failing *load* is non-fatal so a job that cannot read its history still runs. A store value that is not a real state is ignored rather than adopted, so a renamed literal cannot poison every later transition. 6/6 spec-named tests plus the `waiting_user` round trip; the loop test asserts the **exact** state sequence (`idle â€” loading â€” preparing â€” streaming â€” tool_executing â€” iterating â€” streaming â€” done`) and that a declined gate **never** reaches `tool_executing`. Three test failures during the phase were all *my* expectations, corrected at source with the reasoning recorded in the assertion: `idle` has no **direct** edge to `error` (correct â€” nothing can fail before the agent starts, so the invariant is transitive reachability, now a BFS walk rather than a one-hop assertion that would be wrong to require); `done` is two hops from `loading`, not one; and **a terminal machine cannot be reset** â€” `machine.transition('idle')` from `done` is itself an illegal move, so the shared-machine reset silently no-opped and the test was asserting against an empty array. Each loop scenario now uses a fresh machine. 152/152 files, **1927 passed / 6 skipped**, coverage 89.56/82.94/92.88/91.12, `state.ts` 93.1/87.87/95/92.45, `bun run validate` exit 0. **Also recorded: a load-induced flake, not a regression** â€” one full run timed out on `build.smoke.test.ts > builds` at its 180s budget while the machine was loaded (390s total, 14 jsdom environments eating 437s); the same test passes in **40.7s** in isolation and passed in the preceding green full run. > FLAKE RESOLVED (2026-09-18, P-037/P-021): `dependency-cruiser.smoke.test.ts` now shells the `depcruise` CLI child process instead of the in-process `cruise()` (which grew from ~26s standalone to 130s+ in-run inside vitest workers). The child is not CPU-starved by the worker pool and stays in seconds under load. Same config, same tree, same zero-violation assertion â€” fixed at the root, not silenced.

> FLAKE OBSERVED (2026-09-26, P-158/P-160, not fixed â€” it is a machine, not the code): `build.smoke.test.ts > builds` is a real `tsup` + `tsc` run with a 180s budget, and it timed out on two separate full `validate` runs. **Both times it passed in ~40s in isolation** (40.7s and 39.6s against a 180s budget), and the surrounding full run showed 14 jsdom environments consuming 437s and `[vitest-pool]: Failed to start forks worker` â€” i.e. resource exhaustion, not a logic failure. Same class as the P-021/P-037/P-053 load flakes above. Deliberately **not** worked around: raising the timeout to hide a starved machine would mask a real CI problem later. If it ever fails in isolation, treat it as a genuine regression.
>
> ENCODING INCIDENT (2026-09-26, P-160, caused by tooling, now repaired): PowerShell `Get-Content -Raw | Set-Content -Encoding utf8` on an already-UTF-8 file round-trips it through the Windows-1252 ANSI codepage, producing **double-encoded UTF-8** â€” every `â€”` became `Ã¢â‚¬"`. It hit 6 files (`PROGRESS.md` 442 occurrences, plus `agent/hil.ts`, `agent/state.test.ts`, `agent/validation.test.ts`, `agent/hil.test.ts`, `__tests__/build.smoke.test.ts`) and was invisible to `typecheck` and `lint` because the damage landed entirely in comments. **A SECOND, WORSE PASS (2026-09-26, P-161):** the "repair" was then run a second time on a file that was already clean, and because the reverse transform maps *every* character through cp1252, it destroyed the legitimate em-dashes and en-dashes that had just been restored, and turned `Â§`/`Ã—` into `U+FFFD`. 529 characters were affected across 141 lines. Recovered by a Node script that aligns the working file against the clean `git HEAD` copy, copies the committed text for every line it can prove, and applies context rules for this session's own lines â€” with the rule that a `?` becomes `â†’` only when a word or backtick character sits on BOTH sides, which correctly excludes every legitimate question mark (`?ref=`, `?raw`, `??`, `opts?:`, `opts?)`, `config.scanner ?? 'x'`). **RULE: never use PowerShell `Set-Content` to edit a source or plan file in this repo, and never run a whole-file encoding transform speculatively â€” verify the file is actually damaged first, and prefer the `edit` tool or a Node script.** Use the `edit` tool, which round-trips UTF-8 correctly. If a bulk replace is unavoidable, do it in a Node/Bun script instead.
