**P-189 (next):** CLI â€” per P-188's handoff ("Next: P-189 begins CLI"). **READ THE P-189 BLOCK FIRST â€” it has NOT been read, and this note intentionally states only what already-read sources establish; do not execute from this note.** What the repo already tells us: (a) **P-189 opens a different EPIC and a different PACKAGE.** Every phase implemented so far this session is `packages/core` (owner inbesat); `packages/cli` is **aradhy's package** per `project-plans/AGENTS.md` Â§1 ("Never modify `packages/core` if you are aradhy's agent" â€” the mirror holds: core's agent should not casually edit `packages/cli`), and a CLI phase owned by the other developer would sit in their lane. Step 2 of the executor workflow applies here: confirm from the block whether `Owner:` is inbesat or aradhy, and if it is not ours, STOP and surface that rather than editing another developer's package. (b) `packages/cli` already exists and already depends on the core public barrel â€” the whole session's work (`STITCH_ERROR_CODES` now 58, `ProvenanceMap`, `provenanceRoutes`, `generateCredits`, `generateSbom`, `buildChecksumManifest`, `ProvenanceAudit`, â€¦) is exported from `packages/core/src/index.ts` precisely so a CLI can consume it without reaching into internals, and `AGENTS.md` Â§8 names "Importing core internals in cli/web" as a pitfall: import from `@repo-stitcher/core` only. (c) The CLI's stack is fixed: `commander` for commands + `ink` for TUI + **`elysia`** for the HTTP/WS server (per `packages/cli/package.json`'s description and its pinned `"elysia": "1.4.30"`) â€” which is the OTHER half of P-185's ADR-058: core exports framework-agnostic route ARCHITECTORS/descriptors (`provenanceRoutes`, `RouteHost`, `RouteDescriptor`, `RECORDING` host) and **P-193 is where the Elysia adapter binds them**. If P-189 (or a nearby CLI phase) is where that adapter lands, it must live in `packages/cli` and register the core descriptors rather than re-implementing the handlers. (d) The unused exports noted for a CLI: `P-190` was named for `sandbox cache clear` (ADR-051), `P-196`-area for `stitch audit` (ADR-060), and P-204 reuses P-188's committed provenance FIXTURES (ADR-061) â€” so the fixture design decision made in P-188 has a downstream consumer to satisfy. (e) Verify the CLI's own gate before trusting it: `packages/cli` has its own `vitest` config and its own coverage thresholds (AGENTS.md Â§3: cli 70/60/70/70), and the root `bun run test` covers all packages â€” but a known **web** worker-start flake (`packages/web/*`) appears under load and is NOT a real failure.
**P-186 notes (done):** Provenance - Checksum Manifest per `PHASES_DETAILED.md:9351-9394` (Epic 9; Depends On: P-185; Owner: inbesat). Goal: the tamper-evidence anchor â€” SHA-256 of every staged file at completion, persisted, with `verifyChecksums` to detect drift. Files: `provenance/checksums.ts` (new â€” `buildChecksumManifest`, `verifyChecksums`), `checksums.test.ts` (new), `provenance/map.ts` (attach the checksum manifest path). Shapes (verbatim from the block): `ChecksumEntry { path; sha256; sizeBytes }`, `ChecksumManifest { generatedAt; algorithm: 'sha256'; entries; manifestSha256 }`. Steps: walk the staged tree respecting `opts.ignore` globs (`.git`, `node_modules`, `dist`, generated), hashing via a **streaming `createHash`** (bounded memory â€” explicitly not `readFile` whole); sort entries by path; compute `manifestSha256` over the sorted JSON; write `<repoC>/checksums.sha256` in a stable format; `verifyChecksums(repoPath, manifest)` re-hashes each entry and returns `{ valid, mismatches }` with `valid=false` on any mismatch OR missing file; chain into finalization by referencing `manifestSha256` in the git note (P-184) and the SBOM (P-183); parallelise hashing with **bounded concurrency via `p-limit` (P-031)**. **Tests Required (SIX):** `it('builds sorted manifest')`, `it('stable manifest sha')`, `it('writes file')`, `it('verifies clean')`, `it('detects tampered file')`, `it('respects ignore globs')` (temp tree). Acceptance (6): sorted manifest with sha256+size per file, streamed; `manifestSha256` stable over the same tree; `<repoC>/checksums.sha256` written in a stable format; `verifyChecksums` returns mismatches with `valid=false` on drift/missing; ignore globs respected; bounded memory + concurrency-limited. **All inputs VERIFIED to exist â€” no phantom references:** `p-limit` **is** a core dependency (`"p-limit": "7.3.2"`, confirmed in `packages/core/package.json`), so step 4's dependency is real; `util/glob.ts`'s `listFiles(root, patterns, {ignore, dot, signal})` already exists and was used by P-178's full-tree walk (it takes GLOB ignore patterns like `**/node_modules/**`, verified empirically per its header), so the walker needs no new dependency; `node:crypto`'s `createHash` is the house hasher (P-124/`license/generate.ts` has a private `sha256`, `sandbox/cache.ts`/`images.ts` use `createHash` directly) â€” reuse rather than add a fourth. **Watch points:** (a) `manifestSha256` must hash the SORTED JSON of `entries` (P-282 determinism, as P-182/P-183/P-184 all did) and `generatedAt` must NOT be inside that hashed structure or the manifest sha changes every run â€” decide explicitly whether `generatedAt` is hashed and assert it in `stable manifest sha`. (b) "stable format" for `checksums.sha256`: the conventional `sha256sum` line is `<hash>  <path>` (two spaces), which also makes the file verifiable by stock coreutils â€” consider matching it so the artifact is not bespoke. (c) Binary files MUST be hashed as bytes (`git/binary.ts`'s header says checksums "still carry every byte"), so the stream must stay a Buffer stream and never be decoded to utf8. (d) `map.ts` is listed only to "attach checksum manifest path" â€” keep that additive (a field/optional step), not a rewrite of `buildProvenanceMap`, which P-181's suite exercises without a repo (the same call P-181 and P-184 both made about files in their lists). (e) A missing file during `verifyChecksums` is a mismatch, not an error â€” the acceptance says `valid=false` on "drift/missing".
**P-185 notes (done):** `provenance/api.ts` (new): `serializeProvenanceMap`, `provenanceRoutes`, `buildProvenanceRoutes`, `recordingRouteHost`, `PROVENANCE_ROUTES`, `ProvenanceFileDto`/`ProvenanceStatsDto`/`ProvenanceMapDto`, `RouteRequest`/`RouteResponse`/`RouteHandler`/`RouteDescriptor`/`RouteHost`/`ProvenanceRoutesOptions`; `api.test.ts` (new, 5 spec-named); `provenance/index.ts` + `src/index.ts` wiring. **Eleven decisions in ADR-058**, led by **the phase's central conflict**: step 2 types the return as `Elysia.Routes`, but **`elysia` is NOT a core dependency** (verified: core's `dependencies` has none; `packages/cli` pins `"elysia": "1.4.30"`; core's own description reserves the HTTP/WS server for the CLI) - importing it would demand an undeclared dependency (breaking a frozen-lockfile install) AND push a server framework down a layer. So core exports **framework-agnostic descriptors + pure handlers** and the CLI binds them at **P-193**, which the block's OWN step 4 already names and which its test line ("fake ctx/getMap") presumes. `ctx` is typed `RouteHost` so it is **USED** (`ctx.register(...)`) rather than an ignored parameter, and the descriptors are returned too so tests need no server. **P-184 notes (done):** `provenance/gitnotes.ts` (new): `writeGitNotes`, `readGitNotes`, `fetchAllProvenanceNotes`, `attachProvenanceNote`, `buildProvenanceNote`, `mapChecksum`, `mergeNoteFragments`, `DEFAULT_NOTES_NAMESPACE` (`refs/notes/stitch-prov`), `PROVENANCE_NOTE_SCHEMA_VERSION` (1), `DEFAULT_NOTES_TIMEOUT_MS`; `provenance/index.ts` + `src/index.ts` wiring. **Ten decisions in ADR-057**, led by (a) **a write is read â†’ merge â†’ `add -f`, because `git notes add` REFUSES to overwrite** â€” that is exactly why the phase demands append/merge, and a second write is a first-class test; (b) the merge **loses no KEY** (existing-only keys survive; a conflicting scalar takes the NEW value so a re-run can refresh `generatedAt`; nested objects merge recursively); (c) **`mapChecksum` hashes `origins()`** (already path-sorted, P-282) and NOT a `Map`, whose insertion order would make equal content hash differently; (d) the note stays SMALL â€” it references P-183's `SBOM_FILENAMES.cyclonedx` (imported, not retyped) and carries the checksum; (e) **two layers**: the primitive returns a `Result`, and `attachProvenanceNote` degrades a failure to `onWarning` â€” "non-blocking but surfaced" is a property of the wiring, not the primitive; (f) `readGitNotes` distinguishes **absent** (`ok(undefined)`, the normal first-write state) from **corrupt** (`err(GIT_ERROR)`), because folding them together would hide a bad record from the very audit (P-187) that reads them. Also: **no new error code** (`GIT_ERROR`/`CONFIG_ERROR` suffice â€” a note that cannot be written is git failing, not a new failure mode), and **`provenance/map.ts` was deliberately NOT modified** despite being in the file list: `buildProvenanceMap` is pure-ish and unit-tested without a repo, so "attach on finalize" belongs at the pipeline (P-238) via the exported helper â€” the same reasoning that left `agent/gitFacade.ts` untouched in P-181. 5 tests (all spec-named) against **REAL temp git repos**, including the corrupt-note error and both failure layers. **P-173 notes (done):** `sandbox/capture.ts` (new): `CaptureManager` (bounded, timestamped, run-keyed ring buffer + run-scoped artifact extraction + `capture_runs` metadata), `hostArtifactPath`, `sanitizeSegment`, `DEFAULT_LOG_CAPACITY` (10 000), `DEFAULT_MAX_ARTIFACT_BYTES` (100 MB); `SandboxClient.logs()`/`followLogs()`/`RunOptions.onLog` added to P-168's client; new config `sandbox.maxArtifactBytes`. **Six decisions in ADR-046.** (1) **`capture()` is a `Result`** and overflow is `err(SANDBOX_LOG_LIMIT)` (the spec's bare `Promise` shape cannot "raise", and its own "(but keep latest)" says the operation partly succeeded; the retained lines stay on the manager). (3) **`capture_runs` is this phase's own table** created on first use over an injected `DbLike` â€” the `JobRecord` it is destined for is P-239's (forward reference) and `store/schema.ts` reserves its migration slots. **P-170 notes (done):** `sandbox/install.ts` (new): `installDeps(ecosystem, repoPath, opts, client)` + `InstallSpec`/`InstallResult`/`InstallOptions`/`InstallClient`, `countInstalled`, `WORKSPACE_MOUNT`, `NODE_LOCKFILES`, `DEFAULT_INSTALL_TIMEOUT_MS`. **Seven decisions in ADR-043.** (1) **A cross-phase inconsistency fixed:** P-169's own step-1 note requires a *bun-ready* node image, but the committed value was `node:22-alpine` â€” and `bun install` (P-170) / `bun run build`+`bun test` (P-155) cannot run there, so P-171 would have failed. `ECOSYSTEM_IMAGES.node` is now `oven/bun:1.1` (the P-008 base) and the P-169 test expectation was updated. **P-169 notes (done):** `sandbox/images.ts` (new): `ECOSYSTEM_IMAGES` (pinned `node:22-alpine` / `python:3.12-slim` / `rust:1.80-alpine` / `golang:1.23-alpine`), `imageFor`, `isEphemeralTag`, `ephemeralTagFor`, `EPHEMERAL_PREFIX`, `SANDBOX_IMAGES_TABLE_SQL`, `ensureImage`, `ImageClient`/`EnsureImageOptions`; four per-ecosystem Dockerfiles (P-008 base extended) in `sandbox/`; `SandboxClient.buildImage` + a shared `#drainStream` added to the P-168 client; exports wired through `sandbox/index.ts` and the public barrel. **Six decisions in ADR-042.** (1) **The spec's keys were wrong for this codebase:** step 1 sketches `{ npm, python, cargo, go }` but the real `Ecosystem` union is `node|python|rust|go` (`npm`/`cargo` are the *parser* ids) and every consumer (`BuildEcosystem`, `ECOSYSTEM_COMMANDS`, `detectEcosystems`) speaks the latter â€” an `npm`-keyed map is unusable from `run_build`. (5) The `ensureImage` seam is the narrow structural `ImageClient` because `SandboxClient`'s `#private` fields make it nominally typed. (6) The `sandbox_images` table is owned + created on first use (the P-145/P-160 pattern), keyed `ecosystem|tag|platform`, upsert, platform recorded (`null` when unqualified); a missing dockerfile dir/file under `buildLocal` is a typed `CONFIG_ERROR`, never a silent pull. 10 tests (6 spec-named). **P-168 notes (done):** `sandbox/client.ts` (new) is the container-lifecycle wrapper the Sandbox epic builds on: `class SandboxClient` (`isAvailable`/`pull`/`run`/`rm`/`stats`), `ContainerSpec`/`ContainerRun`/`SandboxStats`, `demuxDockerLogs`, `MAX_CONTAINER_LOG_LINES = 500`, `DEFAULT_RUN_TIMEOUT_MS`, `SandboxClient.create()`, plus `sandbox/index.ts` (the sandbox barrel) and public-barrel wiring. **Six decisions in ADR-041.** (1) **`DOCKER_PULL_FAILED` did not exist** and is genuinely distinct from `DOCKER_ERROR` â€” pull failures are image/registry/auth and are what P-175's GH Actions fallback keys off. Added as **code 50** with the same six coordinated edits P-164 used for `TOKEN_LIMIT`, including **P-163's `Record<StitchErrorCode, ErrorRule>`** (whose compile error fired exactly as designed) and `build.smoke`'s count (49â†’50) and `INTEGRATIONS.md` Â§9.1. (2) **`config.sandbox.networkAllow` did not exist**; added as an **optional** boolean (no `defaultConfig` churn; `undefined` = allowed = pre-P-168 behaviour), with the client taking the flag as an option so it is testable without a `Config`. (4) **No casts:** `Docker.createContainer` and `Container.wait`/`logs`/`remove`/`stats` are overload sets and TS checks a one-arg target against the first overload only, so explicit `adaptDocker`/`adaptContainer` adapters name each member â€” measured, not guessed (the same error recurred on `Container.wait`). (5) Timeout â†’ `DOCKER_ERROR`, abort â†’ `ABORTED`; tests narrow on `.code` before `.message` (a union member has no `message` â€” the P-124 lesson). (6) Logs are demultiplexed and bounded to 500 lines/stream; `ContainerRun` mirrors P-155's `SandboxRunResult` shape. 12 tests (7 spec-named). **P-167 notes (done):** `src/agent/__tests__/agent.e2e.test.ts` (new) is the only test that runs the whole agent stack at once: a `runAgentE2E` orchestration harness composes the selection into a git-initialised stage C and drives the real `runToolLoop` with the REAL `GitFacade` (P-165) + `DepsFacade`/`LicenseFacade` (P-166) + a read-only `StagingFacade` + a stub `SandboxRunner` (P-155 seam) + a real `HilQueue` (P-160) + a real `UndoStack` (P-161), with only `MockProvider` (P-144) and the sandbox stubbed. **Six decisions in ADR-040.** (1) Fixtures come from the P-064 generator over COMMITTED `FixtureSpec`s, NOT a committed `fixtures/` tree â€” a committed `.js` tree would be linted by the repo-wide `eslint .` (there is no fixtures ignore) and would need nested `.git` dirs; step 1 explicitly allows "the P-064 generator **or** committed", and a committed spec is still a committed fixture definition. (2) The harness is the orchestrator for what tools deliberately do NOT own: `select_files` only *proposes* (P-148 is read-only), so the harness writes the selection into C. (3) **`fix_dependency` persistence is the caller's job and must MERGE, not replace** â€” P-151 is compute-only ("the caller serializes/writes it, not this tool") and its `serialized` is a synthetic `{name, dependencies}` manifest, so writing it verbatim drops `license` and the compliance gate (P-120) then reads the root package as `UNKNOWN` and BLOCKS an MIT stage. The harness merges `{package, version}` into `fix.section`, and threads `selection` through `runFixDependency` (which exists precisely because `ToolDefinition.execute` has no options channel). (4) The licence test uses the REAL `HilQueue` over a local in-memory `DbLike` and a short `hilTimeoutMs`, so the parked `waiting_approval` request is asserted after `USER_TIMEOUT` without hanging â€” `waitForResolution` leaves the row `pending` â€” and the baseline-only commit log proves C was not finalised. (5) No production `StagingFacade` exists (P-150 specified only the interface); the harness supplies a read-only one over the stage, and the orchestrator (P-238) owns the real one. (6) **Step 6 ("export all agent modules") uncovered a real omission:** `createUndoRecorder` â€” the `UndoRecorder` the loop consumes (P-161) â€” was implemented and used but never exported; added to `agent/index.ts` and the public barrel. 4 spec-named tests (happy end-to-end compose / blocks on license with HIL / caps endless loop / reverts bad edit). Gate: typecheck 0, lint 0, build 0; `test:agent:e2e` 21 files / 160 tests at 84.27/71.98/93.02/88.73 (80/70/80/80 gate OK); full suite **161 files / 2014 tests green**. **Full detail in ADR-040 and the session log above.**
**P-166 notes (done):** `agent/depsLicenseFacade.ts` (new): `class DepsFacade implements ToolContext['deps']` and `class LicenseFacade implements ToolContext['license']` â€” every method a thin delegate, plus factories (`createDepsFacade`/`createLicenseFacade`/`createDepsLicenseFacades`) and `DEFAULT_LICENSE_POLICY`; exported from `agent/index.ts`, and from the public barrel as `AgentDepsFacade`/`AgentLicenseFacade` (the barrel already exports the `ai/types` interfaces â€” the `LicenseCompliancePolicy` precedent). **Seven decisions in ADR-039:** (1) `ai/types.ts` NOT widened â€” the declared contracts are minimal (`analyze`/`install`, `scan`/`check`; only `analyze()` is called by any tool), so the rich verbs are concrete class methods exactly as P-165 kept `commitStage`/`mergeSources`/`stageStatus` off `GitFacade`; no pre-P-166 tool/loop test changed. (2) `analyze()` reads the STAGE's own manifests â€” P-103 `detectEcosystems` â†’ the phase-correct parser (P-104â€“P-107; `parsePythonManifest` is `(filePath, content)`, opposite to its siblings) â†’ flatten every install section to one `name -> range` map; it REJECTS when the stage cannot be walked (both call sites already `try/catch` â†’ `INTERNAL`) but SKIPS an unparseable/stale manifest, because returning `{}` would fake "everything is missing". (3) `install()` is a documented refusal â€” installs belong to the sandbox (P-170) and the host facade never spawns a package manager (ADR-028). (4) Signature reconciliation, module wins over sketch: `resolve(rangeA, rangeB)` (P-109 has no `pkg`), `dedupe(versions, requested)` (P-111's shape), `mergeScripts(a, b, opts)` (P-112 needs prefixes). (5) `complianceGate` fails CLOSED â€” a scan that cannot run is `blocking: true` with the reason â€” and on success runs one honest chain: `scanDeclared` â†’ `detectUnknown` â†’ `checkGpl` â†’ `chooseDualLicense`(OR) â†’ `checkCompatibility` â†’ `evaluatePolicy`. (6) The declared placeholders get real meaning: `scan()` = distinct normalized SPDX ids; `check()` = the gate inverted. (7) `LicenseAggregateFieldsLike` is the ADR-030 structural seam (P-127's input type is module-private). Loop wiring is **injection** (`LoopConfig.context`, P-140/ADR-021) â€” no mock in the production path â€” and the mandated `loop uses real facades` test drives the real `runToolLoop` with a `ToolContext` whose facades are the real classes. 13 tests (4 spec-named). Gate: typecheck 0, lint 0, build 0; full suite 2008/2010 with the **two documented load flakes** (`git/conflict.test.ts` gate timeout + `build.smoke.test.ts` 180s), both green in isolation (64/64, 78s). **Full detail in ADR-039 and the session log above.**
**P-165 notes (done):** `agent/gitFacade.ts` (new) is the *implementation* of the `GitFacade` type P-140 declared and P-161 extended (until now it was only a type) â€” `GitFacade` + `createGitFacade`, `withinRepoRoot`, `parseOriginTrailer`, `StitchOrigin`, `FacadePaths`, `StageCommitOptions`, `CommitInfo`, exported from `agent/index.ts`. **The central correction (ADR-038):** the in-progress draft mapped mutations to source repo `A` (`repoFor(label)`), but ADR-023/025 + P-152 step 3 + this phase's own Context ("child-repo **stage**") require the **child stage C** â€” every mutating tool validates containment against `ctx.config['stageRoot'] ?? 'C'` and passes `'A'` only as the P-140 placeholder (`REVERT_REPO`), and writing to source A would rewrite the user's originals and falsify the P-181 provenance map. So `listFiles`/`readFile` read A/B; `readStagedFile`/`writeFile`/`move`/`deleteFile`/`commit`/`commitStage`/`rollback`/`abort`/`stageStatus`/`mergeSources` act on C. `writeFile` writes the **live stage** and verifies `git add` â€” deliberately **not** P-076 `writeToWorktree`, which mutates an ephemeral detached worktree the next tool (`run_build`, `edit_config`'s read-back) would never see. The **P-082 binary skip is surfaced, never silent:** a NUL in git's 8000-byte window is refused pre-write, and every write is verified with `isBinary` (attr/ext/magic), the bytes reverted and a typed `CONFIG_ERROR` naming the reason returned. `commitStage` = P-077 `commitWithTrailers` + a one-line JSON `Stitch-Origin:` trailer (co-authors too); `rollback` = P-085 `resetTo`, `abort` = P-085 `abortGitOp`; `stageStatus` = P-084 `isClean`; `mergeSources` = P-072 `mergeRepos` (which applies the P-075 resolver). **A real bug the suite caught:** `commitStage` pre-expands the tool's intended files to `git status --porcelain=v1 -z --untracked-files=all` granularity so P-077's file-for-file precheck passes for brand-new dirs, but the first version mishandled a staged **rename** chunk (`R  <new>\0<old>\0`) and passed an already-staged **deletion** to `git add` ("pathspec did not match") â€” fixed by parsing the rename source and excluding already-staged deletions while still letting a genuinely unmatched path fail loudly. The loop's context is injected (`LoopConfig.context`, P-140/ADR-021), so the real facade is the only production implementation and there is no mock in the production path; the full loop E2E is P-167's mandate. 35 tests (6 spec-named: writes and commits / moves files / adds provenance trailers / rolls back / rejects traversal / skips binaries) against real git repos + a real stage. `bun run validate` green: typecheck 0, lint 0, test 159 files / 1997 passed, build 0. **Full detail in ADR-038 and the session log above.**
**Carry forward - the P-161 gap to close when convenient:** `fix_dependency` and `applyProposal` do not emit undo records (P-161 covers only `edit_config` + `move_file`; both return no prior content, so they are deliberately absent rather than recorded dishonestly).
**P-164 notes (done):** The phase that makes the agent's central promise - spend is bounded - actually true. `agent/cap.ts` (new): `LoopCap`, `LoopContext`, `CapStop`, `LoopCapGuard` (`check`/`canRaise`/`raise`), `resolveCap`, `readCapConfig`, `DEFAULT_MAX_ITERATIONS = 20`; new `StitchError` code 49 `TOKEN_LIMIT`; `[agent]` config gains `maxIterations`/`maxTokens`/`maxCostUsd`. **The trap: a guard that never fires.** The loop already had a hard `for` bound and already returned `LOOP_LIMIT`, so a guard layered on top would have been dead code that only ran in unit tests - a spend guard never consulted. The bound was removed, the loop became `for (;;)`, and TypeScript proved the old fall-through unreachable so it was **deleted**. **Off-by-one:** the spec's `iterations > maxIterations` combined with "consult at the top of each iteration" would permit `maxIterations + 1` calls, so the guard refuses on `>=`; `loop.test.ts` passes unchanged, which is the evidence. **`0` means off** (`budgetOf`, one place) - the config defaults are `0`, and read literally every default run would stop at iteration one. **A dollar budget with no priced tracker is a `CONFIG_ERROR`, not a silent no-op**, because the loop's `ModelSpec` has zero pricing. The cap is checked **first** in the body, before the state moves, so no P-162 `state` event is published for an iteration that never ran. **A real defect the tests caught, and it was mine: unbounded `autoRaiseBudget` hung the suite** - a human who always says yes doubles the budget, resumes, hits the new ceiling, and asks again, forever. Bounded by `maxRaises` (default 1), enforced inside the guard; plus two follow-ons (rebuilding the guard reset the counter, and doubling the stop's stale limit made the second raise a silent no-op). **P-163's machinery caught the new code twice**, as designed. **Full detail in ADR-037 and the session log above.**
**P-163 notes (done):** The phase that gives every error a name, a class, a recovery, and a sentence a user can act on. `agent/errors.ts` (new): `ErrorClass`, `classifyError`, `handleError`, `friendlyError`, `fromUnknownThrow`, and an **explicit `Record<StitchErrorCode, ErrorRule>` over all 48 codes** - chosen over the spec's prefix rules because those six rules were measured to cover only **11 of 48**, and because `GIT_ERROR` and `GITHUB_API_ERROR` share a prefix and need different recoveries. Keying by the union makes an unclassified new code a **compile error**. `recoverable = cls !== 'fatal' && cls !== 'config'` spec-verbatim, stated once. `ProviderError` classified from its own `kind` AND its own `retryable` flag. retry -> failover -> abort terminates by construction because budget is an explicit `ErrorBudget` input, so `handleError` is pure and the test drives it to termination with no timer. The loop catch-all around `tool.execute()` is the real acceptance criterion: a tool that *throws* despite its `Result` contract becomes an `INTERNAL` tool result + a `tool_result` reasoning event instead of taking the stitch down - verified with the router wired **and** absent. **No new `StitchError` code** (48 unchanged). Six spec-named tests. **Full detail in ADR-036 and the session log above.**
**P-160 notes (done):** The phase that finally asks a human. `agent/hil.ts` (new): `HilQueue`, `HilRequest`, `HilResolution`, `HilEnqueue`, `HilWaitOptions`, `HIL_TABLES_SQL`, `HilKind`/`HilStatus`. **Four spec conflicts, all in ADR-033.** (1) The Files list names `agent/loop.ts` (line 8075) â€” the **fourth** consecutive Epic 7 phase to do so (P-157:7911, P-158:7968, P-159:8023, P-160:8075); at four occurrences this is a defect in the phase templates, and every one wired into `ai/loop.ts` instead. (2) **"backed by `bun:sqlite` P-230 helper" (line 8070) does not exist** â€” this repo's own PROGRESS describes `db/sqlite.ts` as a *"minimal local engine helper â€” P-230 pending"*, and P-230 is Wave 2. Second forward-reference in two phases after P-159's P-239. So `HilQueue` is `DbLike`-backed and owns `hil_requests`/`hil_resolutions`, created on construction: **not** a `store/schema.ts` migration, because that file's header reserves the extension for P-181/187/**230**/239/241/303/309 and taking a slot another phase owns leaves it with a migration to work around. Same arrangement `ai/audit.ts` has used for `ai_calls` since P-145. (3) `enqueue(req): string` / `resolve(id, res): void` have **no error channel at all**, so neither can honour the no-throw rule; both return `Result`. (4) `waitForResolution`'s "event emitter / polling with interval" offered a choice the spec never made. **The duplicate rule is enforced by the DATABASE, not by application logic** â€” a partial unique index `ON hil_requests (tool_name) WHERE status='pending'`, because two loops racing to gate the same tool would both pass a select-then-delete check and leave two pending rows for one human, and the second approval would then apply twice. The superseded request is marked `cancelled` rather than deleted (it must not vanish from the audit trail) and **its waiter is woken with `ABORTED`** so a loop blocked on it learns it is no longer live instead of waiting out a timeout. **A deferred map, not polling** â€” `Map<id, Deferred>` has no timer to leak, whereas polling has a timer to clear on every exit path and P-156 already showed how that goes wrong when one path is missed; the only timer is the timeout guard, cleared in a `finally`. `Ok` and `Err` are both array-like in neverthrow, so the waiter result is destructured rather than iterated (a type error caught at compile time). **`waitForResolution` consults storage BEFORE it waits**, so an approval the CLI recorded while the loop was between states â€” or before a restart â€” returns immediately instead of hanging. That is what makes the spec's "idempotent via `id`" load-bearing, and it is verified by a test that parks a request, closes the database, reopens it as a "new process", answers, closes again, then reopens a *third* time to show the original wait returns promptly. **`resolve` is idempotent and the first decision stands** â€” a retried or double-clicked approve returns the stored resolution, and a human who approved then accidentally rejected the same card did not change their mind. **A question's answer is the resolution's `note`** (`by` = who, `note` = what they said) rather than widening the frozen `HilResolution` that P-198/P-218 post against; a question approved with no note yields an **empty answer, not a fabricated one**. Recorded in ADR-033 as a judgement call, since adding `answer?: string` is a real alternative. **`cancel` is a third outcome, not a rejection**: `Result<void>`, records *no* resolution so `resolutionFor` stays `null` and nobody can later mistake a cancel for a human saying no, and wakes the waiter with `ABORTED`. **No new `StitchError` code** â€” `USER_TIMEOUT` (nobody answered) and `ABORTED` (someone stopped the wait) already exist and mean exactly this, kept distinct for the same reason P-156 separated them; a stale id from a reconnected web client is `CONFIG_ERROR { field: 'id' }`. Count stays 47. **ADR-029's double-prompt is now CLOSED:** `ask_user` reuses the queue with `kind:'question'` (spec step 4), so the loop no longer asks a human to *approve* the question before the tool asks it â€” one answer, one prompt, one durable channel. The tool layer still knows nothing about the queue, because the loop decorates whatever `HumanResponder` it was handed. **Two real bugs the suite caught, both in my own wiring:** (a) I enqueued *before* `move('waiting_approval')`, so every parked request recorded the previous state (`streaming`) instead of the state it exists to explain â€” the spec's own order is create â€” transition â€” await, and the transition has to come first; (b) the first fix then made `waiting_approval` conditional on a queue being wired, which **regressed P-159's own test** â€” `waiting_approval` is a property of a tool being *gated*, not of how the human is reached, so the transition belongs above the queue/inline-`approvalGate` branch. Both fixed at source, not by weakening assertions. 6/6 spec-named tests; `loop parks then approves` drives the real `runToolLoop`, asserts the machine is genuinely parked in `waiting_approval` with the tool **not** run before answering out-of-band, and also covers the rejected path and the no-queue legacy path. `persists across restart` uses a real on-disk database reopened three times, because "survives a restart" is a claim about a database that an in-memory stub cannot honestly make.

**P-158 notes (done):** The validation layer that stops the model corrupting its own context â€” and the phase where the spec was wrong in a way that would have shipped a **silent hole**. `agent/validation.ts` (new): `validateToolResult`, `sanitizeToolArgs`, `validateToolCall`, `createToolValidator`, `auditValidationFailure`, `MAX_TOOL_RESULT_BYTES = 1_000_000`, `MAX_TOOL_ARGS_DEPTH = 16`, `FORBIDDEN_ARG_KEYS`, `STRICT_PATH_SAFE`. Wired into `ai/loop.ts` before and after `execute` through a structural `ToolValidator` seam in `ai/types.ts` (ADR-030's cycle-breaker, reused). **The decisive finding:** the spec's step 4 reads "after `policy.evaluate` and before `execute`, validate `args`", and the loop already ran P-139's `parseToolArgs` before the policy check â€” so the literal reading is "validate the PARSED args". That is *ineffective*, and the test proved it: **Zod builds a fresh object for a record and therefore strips a `__proto__` key**, so a `{"__proto__":{...}}` payload comes out of `parseToolArgs` clean and the tool executes with the hostile arguments. The scan now runs on the raw `tc.arguments` before any parsing. **A second real bug the suite caught:** `paths: ['a.ts','../bad.ts']` was not rejected â€” the traversal check only handled string-valued path fields, so an array slipped through; path fields are now resolved in both shapes. Denylist is P-152's (`__proto__`/`prototype`/`constructor`), exported as one codebase-wide list. **The walk is iterative with a visit set, not recursive** â€” recursion over model-controlled data is a stack-overflow crash and a cyclic tree would hang a recursive walker forever. Traversal is *resolved*, not grepped: `a/../b` stays inside (accepted), `../b` escapes (refused); backslashes normalized so a Windows-authored `..\..\` cannot slip past. Rejection, not sanitization. Non-serializable results are reported, not thrown. One new code `TOOL_RESULT_TOO_LARGE { size, max }` (46 total); the three arg-side failures reuse `TOOL_ARGS_VALIDATION` with a filterable audit `kind`, because three near-identical codes would violate the no-duplicate-codes rule rather than satisfy it. `ai/audit.ts` gains `AiValidationFailure`, `VALIDATION_TASK`, an additive `validation TEXT` column (the fourth), and `recordValidationFailure()`. **Handoff note honoured:** new P-009 `[agent]` block (`maxToolResultBytes`, `maxToolArgsDepth`), both defaulted so a pre-P-158 config still validates. 6/6 spec-named tests plus an audit-persistence test; the loop test asserts `Object.prototype` is *unmodified* after each hostile input, because a test checking only the return value would pass even if pollution had already happened on the way in. Two of my own expectations were wrong and were corrected with the reasoning recorded in the assertion. 151/151 files, **1921 passed / 6 skipped**, coverage 89.56/82.92/92.84/91.14, `validation.ts` 93.1/88.65/100/97.82, validate exit 0. ADR-031 records all three spec conflicts plus the raw-args decision.

**P-157 notes (done):** The single place autonomy rules live. `agent/policy.ts` (new â€” the spec's "fill stub" does not exist) with `AutonomyPolicy`, `defaultAutonomyPolicy()`, `policyFromAgentConfig()`, `DEFAULT_GATED_TOOLS = ['propose_component','ask_user']`, `ALWAYS_ALLOWED_TOOLS = ['run_build']`, plus `decide`/`effectiveMode`/`withOverride`/`withDeniedTool` (all returning new instances â€” the policy is immutable, so a caller mutating an array it passed in cannot change a live policy's behaviour). `agent/types.ts` gains the spec's `Autonomy`/`AutonomyOverride`/`PolicyDecision`/`AutonomyPolicyConfig`; `evaluate(toolName, toolAutonomy, ctx?)` logs every decision to the P-145 audit trail via `ctx.audit`. Resolution order is **deny list first and absolute** â€” always-allowed â€” per-tool override â€” the tool's own flag â€” `defaultMode`; `auto`â†’`allow`, `gated`â†’`ask`. **The policy is injected, not imported** â€” `src/agent/*` already imports `src/ai/*`, so a direct import into `ai/loop.ts` would close an `agent â€” ai â€” agent` cycle. `ai/types.ts` declares `PolicyVerdict` + `ToolPolicyEvaluator` structurally and `LoopConfig.policyâ†’` is the seam; the agent's `PolicyDecision` matches the shape exactly, so `AutonomyPolicy` satisfies the interface with no import, no cycle, and no `as` cast. **No policy injected â€” P-140 behaviour byte for byte**, so not one pre-existing loop test needed changing. `ai/audit.ts` gains `AiPolicyDecision`, `AiPolicyDecisionRecord`, `POLICY_TASK`, an additive `policy TEXT` column with the same best-effort `ALTER TABLE` migration P-146/P-156 used, and `recordPolicyDecision()` modelled on `recordSwitch` â€” zero token/cost/latency (a policy check calls no model), reason redacted via `redactAuditText` because it embeds a tool name. **No new `StitchError` code** â€” `deny` reuses `TOOL_DENIED` (count stays 45). 6/6 spec-named tests; `loop honors verdict` drives the real `runToolLoop` with a scripted provider so a correct-in-isolation-but-ignored-by-the-loop policy cannot pass. `policy.ts` 92.68/90/91.66/97.22; 150/150 files, 1914 passed / 6 skipped, coverage 89.54/82.88/92.81/91.1, validate exit 0. **Known limitation carried forward:** the policy can now express "allow and let the tool run its own human round-trip", but the shipped defaults leave `ask_user` gated, so it still passes through `approvalGate(name)` first. P-160 owns that consolidation. All three spec-path conflicts recorded in **ADR-030**.
